### Review Findings — review loop 7, production `src/` chunk (2026-07-29)

- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Give grant and remove independent mutation tracking — **RESOLVED (2026-07-29, owner decision): use independent grant/remove generations and cancellation sources.** A grant can start while a removal is only previewed, but the current `CancelRemoveAsync` calls `InvalidateMutations`, cancels the shared grant token, and invalidates its generation. If the server has already accepted the grant, its result and tracking handle are discarded while the grant snapshot can remain stuck at `RequestSent`; the inverse cross-flow cancellation is possible too. Cancelling one flow must affect only that flow, while authorization collapse and component disposal must invalidate both. Existing cross-flow submit guards continue to prevent two commands from being submitted concurrently. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1164]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Preserve authorization-safe member states during paging — `LoadMemberPageAsync` maps every non-usable result, including `Unauthorized`, `NotFound`, and `Invalid`, to `TenantUsersSnapshot.Degraded`, which retains the prior rows. Apply authorization/identity failures directly and retain prior rows only for operational failures; use the same rule for a failed page-one recovery. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:547]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Reject duplicate global-administrator identities before mutation gating — The direct client accepts duplicate `UserId` values and the last-administrator UI guard trusts `Rows.Count`; a duplicated sole administrator therefore appears removable. The aggregate rejects the actual last-admin command, but the BFF/UI evidence still fails open and loses the promised pre-submit hard stop. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:445]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Stop disposing superseded cancellation sources by an arbitrary depth of two — Cancellation is cooperative and does not prove the oldest request has completed, so a third rapid supersession can dispose a token source still used by an in-flight transport and reintroduce `ObjectDisposedException`. Tie disposal to actual load completion or retain sources until component disposal using a bounded-safe ownership mechanism. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:624]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Reset open member mutation flows when the tenant identity changes — `MemberAccessReview.OnParametersSet` clears active flows only when `Members.RequestCursor` changes. Two tenants' first pages both use a null cursor; when the same user exists in each, the old change-role/remove child flow survives route reuse and is rebound to the new tenant without a fresh initiation. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:541]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Retry transient global-administrator notification setup failures — An empty/failed lease is correctly not stored, but `EnsureReadRefreshLeaseAsync` is normally called only on first render. Manual refresh, Retry, Reset, and later successful loads never retry setup, so auto-refresh remains disabled for the circuit. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:815]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Make audit subscription handoff atomic — A tenant change can set `_pendingSubscriptionTenantId` after the active loop reads no pending target but before its `finally`; the `finally` then clears the new target and leaves the selected tenant without notifications. Synchronize/drain the pending target without a lost-update window. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantAuditPage.razor:454]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Track the loaded audit tenant independently from notification subscription state — `OnParametersSetAsync` uses `_subscriptionTenantId` to decide whether the displayed tenant changed. When notification services are absent or return an empty lease, every same-tenant parameter set looks like a tenant change, clears paging/enrichment, and reloads page one. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantAuditPage.razor:317]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Preserve a route back to member page one when bounding cursor history — Keeping only the newest 50 stack entries silently drops the page-one sentinel; after page 51, Previous can return only as far as page two and the pager has no First/Reset action. Preserve the first-page sentinel or provide an explicit page-one recovery control. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:594]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Recover invalid member cursors during notification and command refreshes — `RefreshTenantReadsAsync` assigns an `InvalidCursor` result directly while only button-driven `LoadMemberPageAsync` retries page one, clears history, and announces recovery. Route every authoritative member refresh through the same explicit recovery rule. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:416]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Keep audit/global-administrator cursor bookkeeping aligned with degraded payload pages — A metadata-degraded direct `200` carries the requested page's rows and cursor, but both `IsSuccessfulPage` predicates exclude `Degraded`; the page renders new rows while `_currentCursor` and history still describe the prior page. Commit page state when `RequestCursor` proves the degraded snapshot is the requested payload, while excluding retained prior-page degradation. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantAuditPage.razor:624]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Add global-administrator Previous and failed-Previous regression coverage — Current tests exercise Next and only assert Previous is disabled after notification reset; none prove successful back-navigation or that a failed/superseded Previous preserves history. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1098]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Add tenant-detail notification lease rebinding coverage — Re-render one component from tenant alpha to beta and prove the old lease is released, the new scope is subscribed, and only beta notifications refresh the page. Existing tests cover a route change without notification services and a notification for one fixed tenant separately. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:287]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Add a parent-page post-command detail-refresh regression — Child-flow tests do not prove that `TenantDetailPage` issues the retained-ETag re-query wired through `OnProjectionRefreshRequested` or rejects a late result. Exercise the parent page and assert the second gateway request plus updated overview/configuration evidence. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:387]

### Review Findings — chunk A+B transport/gateway review (2026-07-30)

Review loop 7. Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) over
`8d64563..d377e33` narrowed to chunk A (direct REST transport, DI composition, AppHost) and chunk B (query
gateway, projection-notification refresh) — 7,798 diff lines / 24 files. The full range is ~18,400 lines;
chunks C (state), D (detail/members/audit surfaces), E (global-administrators surface + resx) and F
(integration/evidence) were NOT reviewed in this pass and remain outstanding. The five Story 1.11-attributed
files were excluded. 37 raw findings deduped to 28. Gitlink guard re-run: exit 0, six declared pointers.

#### Decisions resolved — (pending)

- [x] [Review][Decision] Service-discovery endpoint providers are never registered, so all six direct reads throw at send time and `HOST-REF-1` is not actually closed — `.AddServiceDiscovery()` is attached to the read client (`TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:74`), which registers the resolving handler but NOT the endpoint providers. `builder.AddServiceDefaults()` is called by `Hexalith.Tenants.Api`, the EventStore hosts and `Memories.Server`, but **never** by `src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Program.cs` — the project the AppHost launches as `tenants-ui` — and there is no ServiceDefaults project in this repo. Empirically reproduced against the repo's pinned `Microsoft.Extensions.ServiceDiscovery 10.8.0` in production DI shape: every read, including a plain `GET /api/tenants?pageSize=20` against a plain `https://host` base address, throws `InvalidOperationException: No provider which supports the provided service name, 'https://host', has been configured`. The client catches only `OperationCanceledException`/`HttpRequestException`/`IOException` (`TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:197-215`), so this escapes raw: non-search reads fail closed as "unavailable" and the search path tears down the circuit. The three composition tests that "prove" this path register `services.AddServiceDiscovery()` + `AddPassThroughServiceEndpointProvider()` themselves (`TenantsUiCompositionTests.cs:281-282, 419-420, 612-613`), supplying exactly what production lacks. This contradicts AC1, AC7 and the record's `HOST-REF-1 is CLOSED` claim. Fix is architectural and needs an owner call: (a) call `AddServiceDefaults()` in the UI host, (b) register providers inside the module, or (c) drop `.AddServiceDiscovery()` and reject compound schemes — noting `project-context.md` forbids adding reusable hosting plumbing to Tenants. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): option (c).** Drop `.AddServiceDiscovery()` from the read client, narrow the scheme gate to plain http/https, and keep the AppHost-injected resolved endpoint URL (`tenantsApi.GetEndpoint("https")`) — the exact shape the EventStore command/status clients (`:100-101`) and the Memories client (`:115`) already consume successfully with no discovery attached. Rationale: no discovery mechanism is required in this topology because the AppHost supplies the address; Aspire service discovery was introduced by this story and is the direct cause of both this defect and the `%2E` canonicalization defeat below. Option (a) was rejected because Commons' `AddHexalithServiceDefaults` also applies `ConfigureHttpClientDefaults(http => http.AddStandardResilienceHandler())`, injecting silent GET retries and its own timeouts into all six reads and colliding with this story's hand-built linked deadline, fixed failure categories and explicit never-silently-retry invariant. Option (b) was rejected as cross-domain plumbing in a domain module. DAPR service invocation — the correct mechanism for sidecar-to-sidecar traffic — is NOT available today (`tenants-ui` has no sidecar, no app-id and no Dapr package references, and `deploy/dapr/accesscontrol.tenants.yaml` is `defaultAction: deny` allowing only `appId: eventstore` on five POST operations) and is recorded as a separate architectural decision. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:74]
- [x] [Review][Decision] `LifecycleAuthorizationReflection` still resolves from `IHttpContextAccessor`, which is null for the whole interactive circuit — both synchronous reflection properties read `httpContextAccessor?.HttpContext?.User`, so after the first interactive render `Evaluate(null)` returns `Indeterminate` permanently, and `TenantDetailPage.razor:149` gates tenant lifecycle actions as indeterminate for a signed-in global administrator for the rest of the session. This change added `ResolveGlobalAdministratorsAuthorizationAsync` on the same type and migrated the workspace and global-administrators pages to circuit-aware resolution; the tenant-detail consumer was left on the broken path. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): folded into Story 1.11.** Reason recorded for future reviews: Story 1.11 already owns two open principal-resolution decisions (circuit-vs-`HttpContext` precedence, and the single-identity / single-`sub` claim contract), so fixing the resolution seam in one coherent authorization change there avoids two stories making conflicting fixes to the same evaluator. Accepted consequence: tenant lifecycle actions remain gated `Indeterminate` for signed-in global administrators until 1.11 lands. Not actionable inside 1.10. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsBffComposition.cs:21-27]
- [x] [Review][Decision] The host-override availability pairing guard is an XOR on presence only — `hasQueryGatewayOverride != hasReadAvailabilityOverride` never checks that the declared availability agrees with the registered gateway, so a host may register `UnavailableTenantQueryGateway` with `IsConnected: true` and pass composition unchallenged, which is the outcome `ITenantsReadSurfaceAvailability.cs:12-15` says requiring the pair prevents. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): enforce agreement.** The guard must reject a declared availability that contradicts the registered gateway, so the contract enforces what `ITenantsReadSurfaceAvailability.cs:12-15` says it prevents. Accepted consequence: a host that today registers `UnavailableTenantQueryGateway` with `IsConnected: true` starts failing composition — which is the intended fail-closed outcome. See patch item below. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:44-54]

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [from resolved decision, option (c)] Remove Aspire service discovery from the Tenants read client — drop `.AddServiceDiscovery()` at `:74` and the `Microsoft.Extensions.ServiceDiscovery` package reference this story added to `src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.csproj:30`; narrow `IsSendableScheme`/`TryGetHttpBaseAddress` to plain `http`/`https` and reject compound service-discovery schemes with an explicit composition-time reason, retiring the widened-gate rationale and its XML doc at `:130-171`; and rewrite the three composition tests that register `services.AddServiceDiscovery()` + `AddPassThroughServiceEndpointProvider()` themselves (`TenantsUiCompositionTests.cs:281-282, 419-420, 612-613`) so they exercise the real registered shape instead of supplying what production lacks. Note this deliberately reverses the earlier loop's decision to widen the gate for compound addresses; that widening is superseded, not overlooked. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:74]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] [from resolved decision] Make the host-override availability guard reject a contradicting pair — replace the presence-only XOR (`hasQueryGatewayOverride != hasReadAvailabilityOverride`) with a check that the declared `ITenantsReadSurfaceAvailability.IsConnected` agrees with the registered `ITenantQueryGateway`, rejecting `IsConnected: true` paired with `UnavailableTenantQueryGateway` (and the converse) at composition time with an explicit reason. Cover both mismatch directions plus the currently-uncovered `IsConnected: false` override direction. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:44-54]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Reject all-dot route identifiers instead of escaping them — `DangerousDisablePathAndQueryCanonicalization` is defeated in production because the service-discovery resolving handler rebuilds the request URI and re-canonicalizes it. Empirically confirmed at the pinned 10.8.0: `/api/users/%2E%2E/tenants` goes on the wire as `/api/tenants` (the list route), `/api/tenants/%2E%2E` as `/api/`, and `/api/tenants/%2E` as `/api/tenants/`. A user-membership lookup for `..` therefore retargets at the list endpoint; the agent additionally verified that `PaginatedResult<TenantSummary>` deserializes cleanly into `PaginatedResult<UserTenantMembership>` and passes `HasValidPayloadShape`, presenting the caller's whole authorized tenant list as that user's memberships with every role `Unknown`. **Re-measured 2026-07-30 on a clean build reading `RequestUri.PathAndQuery` directly (an earlier measurement round-tripped through `new Uri(string)`, which re-canonicalizes and would have fabricated the result): the service-discovery handler is the SOLE cause.** Without it attached the escaping is preserved on the wire (`/api/users/%2E%2E/tenants` stays intact); with it attached the same path is rewritten to `/api/tenants`. Removing service discovery per the resolved decision above therefore closes the retargeting defect and makes `DangerousDisablePathAndQueryCanonicalization` load-bearing and correct. Keep this patch as defence in depth — reject all-dot and `/`-bearing route values rather than relying on escaping — because the upstream API may still normalize `%2E` during routing, and any future move to DAPR invoke would make a resolved `..` worse (it could traverse out of the `/v1.0/invoke/{appId}/method/` prefix). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:695-728]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Stop an authoritative-search snapshot from being retained and rendered as the ordinary tenant list — search snapshots are built without `RequestCursor`/`RequestPageSize` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:1308-1325`), so they default to `(null, 20)`; on page one at the default page size `MatchesPageScope` and `MatchesRetainedValidator` both pass, `IsRetainableTenantList` accepts `Ready`/`Stale`/`Degraded`/`SearchPageEmpty`, and `RetainTenantList` re-presents the search-filtered subset as the retained authorized list — carrying the search page's `HasMore`, the protected **search** `NextCursor` and `IsAuthoritativeSearch = true` onto the ordinary-list surface. `FallBackFromSearchAsync:1429` guards the reverse direction (`previous?.IsAuthoritativeSearch == false`); the clear-search direction at `:968` is unguarded. Violates AC6 and Story 1.9's KEEP on server-only search cursor state. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:966-968]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Contain per-row detail failures in `EnrichRowsAsync` so one bad row cannot collapse a confirmed list page — the client rejects a `TenantDetail` with null `Name`/`Members`/`Configuration` or a blank member `UserId` as `InvalidPayload` carrying the **raw 200** (`TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:454-458`); `ToEventStoreResult` rewrites only `Unavailable`/`Timeout` to 503, so it throws `EventStoreGatewayException(200)`. The catch filter at `:1826` handles only 403/404/503, so 200 (and 304/400/401) escape, unwind `ListByCursorCoreAsync`, and either re-present the *previous* page as `GatewayUnavailable` or map to `Error` — discarding an otherwise-successful page over a supplementary member-count read. A 401 on one row's detail read likewise renders the whole list as "sign in required". Two guards became dead code as a result (`HasUsableMembers:1504` via `:1813`, and the search-path `detail.Name is null` check at `:1361`) while the comment at `:1811-1812` still asserts the opposite behaviour, and `TenantListReason.RowEnrichmentUnavailable` is now unreachable in production. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1826]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Contain uncontained exception types on the search branch of `ListTenantsAsync` — neither `IsHydrationAvailabilityFailure:1584` nor the filter at `:1092` admits `ArgumentException`, `InvalidOperationException` or `UriFormatException`, and the search branch has no catch-all (unlike `ListByCursorAsync:1640`). A Memories hit with `SourceUri = "tenant:   "` passes the candidate filter at `:1213-1226` (which rejects null, empty and control chars but not whitespace) and then hard-throws `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` at `TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:64`; it escapes `Task.WhenAll`, the gateway and `TenantsWorkspace.razor:790` (which catches only a cancelled `OperationCanceledException`), tearing down the circuit. The `InvalidOperationException` from the service-discovery decision item travels the identical route. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1092]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Stop funnelling `InvalidPayload` (200) and `InvalidMetadata` (304) through HTTP-status exception mapping — the effective-status override at `:2296-2299` covers only `Unavailable`/`Timeout`, so both reach the mappers as non-error statuses. `MapDetailException`'s default arm (`:2571`) returns `Degraded(null, …)` and `MapUserTenantException`'s (`:2587`) returns `Degraded([], GatewayFailure)` — degraded over zero retained rows on a first load, the exact defect this story fixed for tenant-users at `:2016-2023`. `MapUserTenantException` also has no 404 arm, so `NotFound` lands there too. Neither 200/304 status is in `IsRetainableReadFailure`'s excluded set (`:2076`), so with a matching `previous` they re-present confirmed rows as "last confirmed remain visible" on a corrupt payload. Mutation-verified: making `ToEventStoreResult` treat `InvalidMetadata`/`InvalidPayload`/`Unknown` as success keeps 1,565/1,565 green — no gateway test drives those categories through it. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:2291-2313]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Observe the fail-closed read-surface flag through the composed container — mutation-verified: flipping `TenantsReadSurfaceAvailability(IsConnected: false)` to `true` in the no-base-address branch keeps all 1,565 UI tests green. The two tests naming this behaviour (`TenantsUiCompositionTests.cs:107`, `:115`) construct `TenantsBffComposition` directly with a hand-built availability record and never call `AddHexalithTenantsUiModule`; `:351` asserts only the `ServiceDescriptor`. Only the connected branch is asserted through a real container. The flag gates the grant read-surface guard (`GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:994`) and the notification leases (`TenantDetailPage.razor:351`, `TenantAuditPage.razor:469`). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:89-90]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Exercise the global-administrators page's new async authorization resolution — `GlobalAdministratorsPageTests.StubTenantsBffComposition` (`:1650-1661`) never overrides `ResolveGlobalAdministratorsAuthorizationAsync`, so every GA page test hits the default interface implementation, which is `ValueTask.FromResult(GlobalAdministratorsAuthorizationReflection)` — the old synchronous claims property. Mutation-verified: swapping the call back to the synchronous property keeps 1,565/1,565 green, so the page could silently revert to claims-only authorization, and the fail-closed `catch { return Indeterminate; }` branch is unreachable in test because the default impl cannot throw. `TenantsWorkspaceTests.cs:671` does override it; the two stubs are inconsistent. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1340]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Retire the `RestQueryClientAdapter` seam's fabricated expectations — `grep 'SubmitQueryRequest\|IEventStoreGatewayClient' TenantQueryGateway.cs` returns nothing: the contract those assertions check exists only in the harness, which hardcodes `"system"`, `"index"`, `"global-administrators"`, the `QueryType`/`ProjectionType` constants and the cursor/pageSize payload (`:5894-5990`), re-implements freshness resolution (`:6001-6013`) and fixes `FailureKind.None` (`:6018`). 61 assertions on `Request.QueryType/AggregateId/EntityId/Payload` plus 4 `tenantSubstituteQueries` guards cannot fail for any production change; 154 tests run through this seam versus 18 through the real typed-client substitute. Mutation-verified corollary: reverting `ToReasonCode` to `failureKind.ToString()` — the defect its own XML remark describes — fails only 2 of 1,565 tests, because the GA and audit page-one recovery paths still see only hand-built `EventStoreGatewayException(400, …, reasonCode: "invalid-cursor")`. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:5887-6021]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Verify the returned tenant identity in `LoadTenantDetailAsync` — it returns `result.Payload` unchecked, and `EnrichRowsAsync` then writes `detail.Members.Count` and the owner count onto a row whose identity came from the *list* summary, so a cache/proxy mix-up, a server routing defect, or the route confusion above displays tenant B's member and owner counts on tenant A's row with no signal. Every sibling call site verifies identity (`:191`, `:1351`, `:1873`) and the audit read got a dedicated cross-tenant guard inside the client (`TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:144-154`); this is the one call site left out, and no test covers it. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1836-1842]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Guarantee `_running.Remove(key)` in `RunRefreshLoopAsync` with a `finally` — `OnProjectionChanged` starts the loop only when `_running.Add(key)` succeeds, and removal happens on exactly one path (the normal return inside the lock). Any throw from the loop body outside the inner `try` — most plausibly `LogReason` on a logger from a torn-down circuit scope — leaves the key in `_running` forever, so every later notification sees `start == false` and auto-refresh never restarts. The task is fire-and-forget (`_ = RunRefreshLoopAsync(key)`), so the fault is unobserved. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/TenantReadRefreshSubscription.cs:242-271]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Give the tenant-users read invalid-cursor page-one recovery — `MapTenantUsersResponse` maps `InvalidCursor` to a terminal `TenantUsersSnapshot.Invalid(...)` with no rows and no retry, while all five sibling reads restart at page one and say so (`:551-563`, `:736-749`, `:788-808`, `:1600-1629`). After a DataProtection key rotation or cursor-scope change the member surface stays unusable until paging state is cleared by hand. Separately `isListRefreshed` is passed `false` at the only call site (`:351`), so `TenantUsersReason.ListRefreshed` at `:2060` can never be produced. The `InvalidCursor` kind was introduced by this change precisely to enable that recovery. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1971-1972]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Bind the global-administrators `304` version promotion to `Current`, and stop inheriting lifecycle across a promoted version — `notModifiedFreshness` may resolve to `Stale` (`IsSupportedNotModified` accepts `Current or Stale`), yet `ProjectionVersion = result.Metadata?.ProjectionVersion` is assigned unconditionally at `:642`; only `IsCompleteEvidence` is gated on `Current`. Review loop 5 authorized promotion only for an exact strong-ETag **current** `304`. Separately `ResolveNotModifiedLifecycle:2379-2390` carries `previous.Lifecycle` forward whenever the `304` omitted the lifecycle header — a real wire state, since `RestApiControllerEmitter.cs:467` emits it only when it normalizes to non-`Unknown` while `:477` emits `X-Hexalith-Is-Stale` whenever staleness is known — so `IsCompleteEvidence` can become true at a projection version whose lifecycle was never asserted, and that flag is what makes "this user is not a global administrator" conclusive. No test pins either polarity. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:621-648]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Bound the tenant-list enrichment fan-out and configure a read timeout — `EnrichRowsAsync` issues up to `MaximumPageSize = 100` strictly sequential detail reads per list render, and the client registration sets only `BaseAddress`, so `HttpClient.Timeout` is the 100 s default which `TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:178-181` adopts as the whole-read deadline. A slow or hung Tenants API blocks one list render for up to ~100 × 100 s, and `TenantReadRefreshSubscription` can retrigger it on every projection notification. Search hydration bounds itself at `MaximumHydrationConcurrency = 8`; the ordinary list has no concurrency, batching or timeout bound. Epic 1 targets ~one-second warm list reads. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1793-1834]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Guard a blank tenant id on the detail read instead of reporting an outage — `GetTenantAsync` has no blank-id guard, unlike tenant-users (`:303-305`) and audit (`:780-782`), so `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrWhiteSpace` throws inside the client, is swallowed by the generic handler at `:266`, and returns `TenantDetailSnapshot.Unavailable(...)` plus a spurious `DirectTenantsReadFailed` warning — a malformed request reported as the API being down, in the UI and in the operational log. `TenantDetailPage.razor:307` builds the request straight from the route value, so `/tenants/%20` reaches it. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:101-110]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Collapse the duplicated freshness resolver — no production code reads `TenantsRestQueryResponse.Freshness` on the 200 path; the gateway recomputes freshness with its own `ResolveFreshness` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2332`), which is what actually feeds every snapshot and the rendered stale/current banners. Mutation-verified: hard-coding the OK-path `Freshness` to `Unknown` fails exactly 2 tests, both in `TenantsRestQueryClientTests`, so the suite reads as if it pins the rendered freshness while the rendered value comes from a second, independently-maintained implementation. Either have the gateway consume the response value and delete its duplicate, or drop the member and assert client freshness only where it is load-bearing (the `304` gate). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:312-318]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Require a non-blank request cursor before page-one recovery on the list and audit reads — both enter recovery on the invalid-cursor sentinel with no `!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(request.Cursor)` guard (the user-tenants and global-administrator siblings carry it at `:551` and `:738`), then unconditionally stamp `TenantListReason.ListRefreshed` / `TenantAuditSurfaceKind.ListRefreshed` — telling the operator paging restarted from page one for a load that never paged. `IsInvalidAuditCursor` also matches the sentinel loosely across `Reason`/`Title`/`Type`/`Detail` (`:2619-2625`). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1600-1614]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Map an unusable route identifier to `InvalidRequest`, not `Unavailable`/503 — `TryEscapeRouteValue` failure (an id containing `/`) returns `Failure(Unavailable, 503)`, so the gateway raises `SignalReadFailure(..., Unavailable)` and maps 503 to `Unavailable` snapshots: a caller-side input defect is indistinguishable from the Tenants API being down, in the UI and in the log. `InvalidRequest` exists in the same enum for exactly this class. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:695-712]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Compute the detail read's conditional validator after the tenant-identity check — `string? requestValidator = request.ETag ?? previous?.ETag;` at `:112` runs before `HasSameTenantDetail(previous, request.TenantId)` at `:122`, so on a route change tenant alpha's projection-wide ETag is sent as `If-None-Match` on tenant beta's read. Acceptance is correctly gated (unconditional retry at `:124`), so this costs a wasted round trip rather than a wrong snapshot, but it is the send-side pattern the review-loop-4 patch forbids and the tenant-users read (`:317`) does not do it. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:112]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Normalize page size consistently across the six reads — `CanonicalizeListRequest` clamps to 1..100 for the list read only; the other five pass it through, and `BuildUri` always emits `pageSize` because the property is a non-nullable `int`, so `pageSize=0` reaches the service verbatim and returns a 400 that renders as `Invalid`/`GatewayFailure` — an operator-facing invalid-request state for a value the list read silently normalizes. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:1097-1098]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Delete or annotate two unreachable ETag guards — `requestValidator is { IsWeak: false }` (`:411`) and the `tag.Length > MaximumETagValueLength` bound (`:637`) are unreachable by construction: `NormalizeValidator:604` rejects any value containing `"` and emits only strong validators, and the raw-header bound at `:626` already caps the trimmed tag. Mutation-verified: relaxing `:411` to `is not null` fails 0 of 1,565 tests, and `Not_modified_requires_strong_etag_...`'s `strongETag: false` row actually passes on the response-side `metadata.ETag is not null` check. No behaviour is at risk; the risk is reading the passing tests as evidence a security-adjacent validator rule works. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:411]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Make a `CancelAfter` regression fail red instead of hanging — mutation-verified: removing the linked transport deadline makes `Linked_transport_deadline_covers_success_and_problem_details_bodies` block indefinitely (killed at 70 s), because the test's blocking stream awaits only the token production supplies. Under `-parallel none` with no per-test timeout that surfaces as a stuck CI job with no attribution. Wrap the read in an outer `WaitAsync` or add a per-test timeout. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClientTests.cs:872]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Add the missing XML documentation on two new internal types — `InvalidCursorSignal` (internal enum, no `<summary>` on the type or any of its four members) and `ResponseContentTooLargeException` (internal exception, no docs). The Hexalith baseline requires XML docs on internal members, every other new type in the same folder is documented, and this story already recorded the same item as resolved. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/InvalidCursorSignal.cs:3-9]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [low] Use round-trippable ETag literals in the gateway conditional-read tests — `NormalizeValidator` returns `null` for any ETag containing `"` and `GetStrongETag` returns the unquoted tag (pinned at `TenantsRestQueryClientTests.cs:206`), yet every gateway `304` test models the retained validator as a quoted literal (e.g. `TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:1876`, `:1898`). Against the real client that value would send no validator and the `304` path would be unreachable. The gateway only does string equality so there is no production defect, but nothing proves the retained ETag the gateway stores is round-trippable through the real transport. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:1876]

### Review Findings — chunk C+D+E state/surface review (2026-07-30)

Review loop 7, second pass. Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap,
acceptance-auditor) over `8d64563..d377e33` narrowed to chunk C (`State/**` snapshots + state tests),
chunk D (`TenantDetailPage`, `MemberAccessReview`, `TenantAuditPage`, user panels + surface tests) and
chunk E (`GlobalAdministratorsPage` + `.razor.css` + `.resx`/`.fr.resx` + page tests) — 7,546 diff lines /
38 files. Chunk F (integration tests, Aspire topology, route smoke, evidence/provenance docs) was NOT
reviewed in this pass and remains outstanding. The five Story 1.11-attributed files were excluded; the
global-administrators files declared by both stories were included. 44 raw findings deduped to 41.

The verification-gap layer ran 40 production mutations in Release against the full 1,565-test UI suite; 19
were killed and 21 survived. Every mutation was reverted and the tree re-verified clean and green.

HEAD advanced from `d377e33` to `b045129` during the pass (a concurrent session committed the pending
review docs and the Builds/EventStore pointer bumps). `git diff d377e33 b045129 -- src tests` is empty, so
all four layers reviewed byte-identical code. Gitlink guard re-run at `b045129`: exit 0, six declared
pointers.

#### Decisions resolved — 2026-07-30

- [x] [Review][Decision] [high] An open member command flow does not survive its target row leaving the page, and the pager never recovers — `ActiveChangeRoleMember`/`ActiveRemoveMember` resolve against `Members.Rows` (`MemberAccessReview.razor:355-356`), so a successful removal whose confirmation refresh re-reads the *same* cursor unmounts the `@if`-guarded flow region and destroys its lifecycle state, receipt and pending projection confirmation. `_activeRemoveMemberUserId` is cleared only when `Detail.TenantId` or `Members.RequestCursor` changes (`:541`), neither of which did, so `HasOpenMemberCommandFlow` stays `true` and Next **and** Previous are disabled permanently with no rendered flow to close and no reset control — only a full page reload recovers. Empirically reproduced with a temporary test (since reverted): after re-rendering with the same tenant/cursor minus the target row, `tenants-remove-member-flow` is absent and `tenants-member-next` still carries `disabled`. The loop-7 patch at `:541` closed the *paging* trigger for this hazard and left the *refresh* trigger open; `TenantDetailPage.RefreshTenantReadsAsync:503` assigns the member result unconditionally, with none of the terminal/usable classification `LoadMemberPageAsync:614-627` applies. The two symptoms pull in opposite directions and cannot both be fixed without an owner call: (a) render each flow from its own captured intent rather than from `Members.Rows`, so a disappearing row is the *confirmation* and the receipt survives, and release `HasOpenMemberCommandFlow` once the flow is terminal; (b) clear the active-flow ids when the target row is absent, accepting that the receipt is lost; or (c) keep the flow mounted but exempt terminal flows from the pager gate only. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): option (a).** Render each flow from its own captured intent. Rationale: the row disappearing *is* the successful removal, so keeping the flow mounted lets it render the Confirmed receipt at the moment that becomes true, which is what the "accepted / projection-confirmed / audit-available are distinct" rule asks for. It also fixes the deadlock as a side effect — the flow stays rendered, so Close exists, so the id is cleared normally — and closes the merged refresh-teardown finding in the same change. The confirmation path already works off `RemoveMemberProjectionEvidenceProvider` (a tenant-**detail** read) while the unmount is driven by the tenant-**users** read, so the confirmation logic itself needs no change. Option (b) was rejected because it buys the deadlock fix by destroying the receipt at exactly the moment it matters and makes teardown the policy, undoing the intent of the loop-7 patch at `:541`. Option (c) was rejected because it does not stop the unmount on its own and would still need (a), leaving two mechanisms tracking one concept. Accepted consequence: the flow renders a member record no longer present in the current page, so the `Detail`/`SurfaceKind`/`Freshness`/friction props passed into it must stay coherent for a row that has left the surface. See patch items below. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:355-356]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] Grant and remove projection confirmation are structurally unreachable once the administrator list exceeds one page — both requery helpers re-read page one only (`requestedCursor: null, useRequestedCursor: true`, `PageSize = 20` at `:473`). `GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot.ConfirmProjection` concludes success from absence only when `snapshot.IsCompleteEvidence` (`GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot.cs:170`), and `IsCompleteGlobalAdministratorsEvidence` requires `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(request.Cursor) && !payload.HasMore` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2214-2218`) — with 21+ global administrators `HasMore` is always true on page one, so **every** successful removal terminates at `UnableToVerify` with "Current complete projection evidence is required before confirming global administrator removal." Grant fails the same way: `ConfirmProjection` needs the granted row present in the page-one payload (`GlobalAdministratorGrantCommandSnapshot.cs:155`), and a user id sorting past position 20 is never there. Both fail closed, but the story's projection-confirmed success state becomes unattainable on any multi-page deployment and the operator gets a permanent false negative instead of an honest "not on this page". Options: (a) accept it and reword the terminal state to say the evidence is page-scoped rather than implying failure; (b) page forward until the target is found or the list is exhausted, bounded; (c) add a targeted single-administrator read — which the **Block If** clause makes a public-contract change and therefore blocking. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): option (a) now, with option (c) recorded as the real fix for a follow-up story.** Reword the terminal state so it says the evidence is page-scoped rather than implying the command may have failed, and give it its own reason code distinct from a genuine verification failure. Rationale: zero behavioural risk, none of the fail-closed gates move, and the audit trail — the story's authoritative evidence path — is unaffected. Option (b) was rejected as the riskiest of the three despite looking the most "proper": proving absence requires exhausting the list, and because each read carries its own independent ETag, freshness and projection version, "absent from all pages" means proving every page was mutually current and version-consistent, with a mid-walk version bump invalidating the whole traversal. That is a completeness proof re-derived across N independently-versioned reads to confirm a **destructive** action, where a subtle composition bug tells an operator a global administrator was removed when they were not. Option (c) is correct and O(1) but is blocked inside this story by the **Block If** clause. Accepted consequence: on deployments with 21+ global administrators the operator confirms removals via the audit trail rather than in-surface, until the targeted read lands. See patch items below. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1573-1592]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] The authentication-change path re-authorizes from raw claims, bypassing the principal resolver every other path uses — `ApplyAuthenticationStateChangedAsync` calls `TenantsGlobalAdministratorClaims.Evaluate(authenticationState.User)` directly and, on `Authorized`, sets `_authorizationReflection = Authorized` and reloads. Every other path (`OnInitializedAsync`, `ReauthorizeAsync`, therefore every load, `SubmitGrantAsync`, `SubmitRemoveAsync`, both status refreshes) goes through `BffComposition.ResolveGlobalAdministratorsAuthorizationAsync()`, which delegates to `ITenantConfigurationPrincipalResolver` when one is registered and consults the claims property **only** when it is null (`TenantsBffComposition.cs:30-32`). A principal the resolver would classify `Indeterminate`/`MissingPermission` therefore becomes `Authorized` after any `AuthenticationStateChanged` notification, unlocking the grant/remove surfaces for the rest of the circuit subject only to the freshness gates. `ReauthorizeAsync()` exists at `:1352-1363` and is used everywhere else, so the consistent fix is one line — but the event handler receives a *fresh* principal while the resolver may read a staler source, and Story 1.11 already owns the circuit-vs-`HttpContext` precedence decision on this exact evaluator. Owner call: route this path through the resolver like every other, or keep the fresh-principal shortcut and document why it may diverge. **RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (owner decision): option (c) — folded into Story 1.11, deferred from 1.10.** Rationale: option (a)'s correctness depends entirely on how 1.11 resolves circuit-vs-`HttpContext` precedence, so picking it now means picking blind — and the chunk A+B review established that `LifecycleAuthorizationReflection` on this same type returns `Indeterminate` permanently on an interactive circuit because `HttpContext` is null. If `TenantConfigurationPrincipalResolver` shares that weakness, routing this path through it trades a fail-open for a fail-shut. Story 1.11 already owns both open principal-resolution decisions on this evaluator (circuit-vs-`HttpContext` precedence, and the single-identity / single-`sub` claim contract), and the chunk A+B review folded the structurally identical `TenantDetailPage` item there four days ago for the same stated reason: avoid two stories making conflicting fixes to the same evaluator. Option (b) was rejected as documenting a known fail-open rather than fixing it. Accepted consequence: until 1.11 lands, an `AuthenticationStateChanged` notification can set `_authorizationReflection = Authorized` for a principal the strict resolver would classify `Indeterminate`/`MissingPermission`, unlocking the grant/remove surfaces for the rest of that circuit subject to the freshness gates. Not actionable inside 1.10. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1279]

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [from resolved decision, option (a)] Render member command flows from their own captured intent — capture the `TenantMember` when a change-role or remove flow opens and pass that captured record into `ChangeTenantMemberRoleFlow`/`RemoveTenantMemberFlow`, instead of re-resolving `ActiveChangeRoleMember`/`ActiveRemoveMember` against `Members.Rows` on every render (`:355-356`). The flow must survive its target row leaving the page so the Confirmed receipt renders at the moment the removal succeeds, and so the Close button stays available to clear `_activeRemoveMemberUserId` — which is what breaks the permanent pager deadlock at `:251`/`:257`. Keep the existing `OnParametersSet:541` reset on tenant/cursor change. Ensure the `Detail`, `SurfaceKind`, `Freshness` and `TargetGlobalAdministratorFriction` props remain coherent for a member no longer present in the rendered page. Also classify the member result on the main path of `TenantDetailPage.RefreshTenantReadsAsync` (`:503`) the way `LoadMemberPageAsync:614-627` does, so a refresh that returns `Unauthorized`/`Error` does not silently empty the surface — today that classification exists only inside the `InvalidCursor` recovery branch at `:475-491`. Add regression coverage for a same-tenant/same-cursor row disappearance: assert the flow stays mounted, reaches its terminal receipt, and both pager buttons recover. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:355-356]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] [from resolved decision, option (a)] State page-scoped global-administrator evidence honestly instead of implying failure — `GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot.ConfirmProjection` currently returns `UnableToVerify` with "Current complete projection evidence is required before confirming global administrator removal." whenever `IsCompleteEvidence` is false, which on 21+ administrators is *every* removal. Give the page-scoped case its own reason code and copy saying the page-one evidence cannot settle the question and directing the operator to the audit trail, distinct from a genuine verification failure. Apply the same distinction to `GlobalAdministratorGrantCommandSnapshot.ConfirmProjection` (`GlobalAdministratorGrantCommandSnapshot.cs:155`), where a granted user id sorting past position 20 is never on page one. Add EN/FR resource strings and cover both the complete-evidence and page-scoped branches. Do not change any fail-closed gate. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/State/GlobalAdministrators/GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot.cs:170]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Stop binding tenant-detail notification setup to the load cancellation token, and retry it — `OnParametersSetAsync` subscribes at the end of the initial load and passes `cancellationToken`, the token every later load cancels via `BeginLoad()` → `ReplaceActiveCancellation()`. A member pager click, any `RefreshTenantDetailAsync` from a command flow (`:135`, `:152`, `:200`), or a second `OnParametersSetAsync` arriving while `SubscribeAsync` is awaiting cancels it; `catch (OperationCanceledException) when (cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested) { return; }` then leaves `_readRefreshLease` and `_subscriptionTenantId` null. `SubscribeAsync` has exactly one call site, inside `OnParametersSetAsync`, so nothing retries for the same tenant and AC5 projection auto-refresh is dead for the rest of the circuit — only a route change to a *different* tenant restores it. The `!lease.IsSubscribed` arm at `:378-382` reaches the same dead end while its comment claims recording it "would block every later attempt", which is true only because no later attempt exists. Both sibling pages already carry the fix: `TenantAuditPage.razor:398-406` passes `CancellationToken.None` with a comment naming this exact defect, and `GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:819-821` passes `None` **and** re-attempts from `OnAfterRenderAsync`. Found independently by two layers. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:356-382]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Admit `Unknown` freshness to `IsUsableMemberPage` — a good members page whose projection-lifecycle headers are absent or degraded resolves to `Freshness = Unknown`, which `TenantUsersSnapshot.Ready` maps to `Kind = Unknown` (`TenantUsersSnapshot.cs:40-46`). `IsTerminalMemberPage` rejects it (Unauthorized/NotFound/Invalid only) and `IsUsableMemberPage` accepts only `Ready or Empty or Stale` plus cursor-matched `Degraded`, so control falls to `TenantUsersSnapshot.Degraded(tenantId, retained, …)`, which re-presents the **previous** page's rows and `HasMore`/`NextCursor` and leaves `_memberCursor`/`_memberCursorHistory` untouched. Next stays enabled, so every click repeats the round trip and repaints page 1 labelled "Member evidence is degraded" — the operator can never leave page one and the state message is false, because the read succeeded. Every sibling predicate written in this same change includes `Unknown`: `TenantQueryGateway.IsRetainableTenantUsers:2106-2111`, `IsUsableGlobalAdministratorsPage:2220-2225`, `MemberAccessReview.CanOpenMemberAudit:403-406`, `GlobalAdministratorsPage.IsAppliedRequestedPage`. The same hole applies to the invalid-cursor recovery at `:597` and `:479`. Zero coverage: `grep -rn "TenantUsersSurfaceKind.Unknown" tests/` returns one hit, a pure state-mapping unit test. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:725-731]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] Pin all three projection-evidence clauses of `ActionsAreEvidenceBacked` — this is the gate that authorizes member change-role and remove. Three independent mutations each survive the full 1,565-test suite: removing the members-side `Lifecycle is ProjectionLifecycleState.Current`, removing the detail-side one, and removing `!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(ProjectionVersion)`. A projection-backed `200` carrying only the legacy `X-Hexalith-Is-Stale: false` resolves to `Freshness = Current` with `Lifecycle = Unknown` (`TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:335,377-396`), so without the lifecycle clauses sensitive member actions become available on evidence that is not projection-confirmed; `projectionVersion` is `null` whenever the header is absent, so without the blank-version clause `string.Equals(null, null)` makes "version-consistent" true for two reads that state no version at all. The XML doc on `GlobalAdministratorsSnapshot.cs:166-167` cites this member gate as the equivalent of the GA gate — and the GA side is killed by five tests while the member side is killed by none. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:408-416]

The only assertions on `[data-testid='tenants-detail-member-summary']` are `TenantDetailSurfaceTests.cs:90` and `:802`, both `ShouldContain("2 members")` — and the stub localizer at `:2423-2424` defines both variants with that same `"{0} members"` prefix, so the assertion is a substring match that cannot distinguish the branches. Assert the full rendered string per branch. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:247-268]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Preserve authorization-safe member absence at non-`Current` freshness — `TenantUsersSnapshot.Empty` selects `freshness == Stale ? Stale : freshness == Unknown ? Unknown : Empty`, discarding the `Empty` kind whenever freshness is not current. `MemberAccessReview` then takes the `Rows.Count == 0` branch and renders `Tenants.Members.State.Title` = "Member read unavailable" with "Member evidence is stale", telling the operator the read failed when it succeeded and authoritatively returned nothing. `IsAuthorizationScopedEmpty` is set by the factory and has **zero** consumers under `Components/` — only the `Empty` kind reaches the `Tenants.Members.Empty.Title`/`.Message` copy ("No visible members … does not reveal hidden memberships"). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/State/TenantUsers/TenantUsersSnapshot.cs:62-86]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Reset `PagingRecovered` when a member snapshot is retained — `TenantUsersSnapshot.Degraded` returns `previous with { Kind, Freshness, Lifecycle, Reason, IsRefreshing }` and `Refreshing` returns `previous with { IsRefreshing = true }`; neither clears `PagingRecovered`. After a page-one recovery stamps it (`TenantDetailPage.razor:602-606`), the next transient failure with a matching page scope re-presents the flag, and `MemberAccessReview.razor:58` renders its `role="status" aria-live="polite"` notice on that flag alone — announcing "the list restarted at page one" for a read that restarted nothing and in fact failed. The GA retention path explicitly resets its sibling flag (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2203` writes `IsCompleteEvidence = false`) and `GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:908-913` re-runs `_cursorHistory.Clear()` on an inherited `PagingRecovered`, so the retention-resets-derived-flags idiom exists and is simply not applied here. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/State/TenantUsers/TenantUsersSnapshot.cs:89-107]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Restore member paging state after a detail-only refresh — `BeginLoad()` unconditionally sets `_memberPageLoadInFlight = false` and strips `IsRefreshing` off `_tenantUsersSnapshot`, then cancels the previous token. `RefreshTenantDetailAsync` (wired to `OnProjectionRefreshRequested` for the metadata, configuration and lifecycle flows at `:135`, `:152`, `:200`) calls `BeginLoad()` and never re-establishes either. So a metadata command confirming mid-flight cancels an in-progress member page read, hides the refresh indicator, re-enables the pager and leaves the table on the *old* page with no error and no retry; the superseded load's `finally` at `:718-721` cannot restore anything because `generation == _loadGeneration` is false. `RefreshTenantReadsAsync:439` restores `TenantUsersSnapshot.Refreshing(retainedMembers)` immediately after `BeginLoad` for exactly this reason; `RefreshTenantDetailAsync:389-411` does not. Defeats the stated "suppress duplicate paging requests while a load is active" invariant, since the pager's `Disabled` expression reads `Members.IsRefreshing`, not `_memberPageLoadInFlight`. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:759-769]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Generation-guard the global-administrators Retry and Reset paths — `RetryAsync:929` and `ResetPagingAsync:932` neither set nor read `_pageLoadInFlight`, and `ResetPagingAsync` clears `_cursorHistory`/`_currentCursor` synchronously *before* awaiting, so the mutation is not generation-guarded. With a `Stale` rows-bearing snapshot both Retry/Reset (via `CanRecover`) and the pager (via `ShouldRenderRows`) render simultaneously with no `Disabled` binding on the former. Click Next then Reset: Next's `LoadAsync` returns `null` as superseded, its `finally` re-enables the pager while `_snapshot` is still the pre-reset page-3 object, and a second Next pushes the now-`null` `_currentCursor` as `priorCursor` — history records page one for a view showing page 4 and Previous jumps the operator back silently. The added regression tests cover Next/Previous and notification supersession but never a Reset overlapping a page load. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:929-940]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Marshal the cursor-history writes on the audit and global-administrators pagers — `NextPageAsync` does `await LoadAsync(...).ConfigureAwait(false)`, which always suspends because it performs the HTTP read, so `_cursorHistory.Push(priorCursor)`, `_currentCursor = nextCursor` and `finally { _pageLoadInFlight = false; }` all run on a thread-pool thread concurrently with the render `LoadAsync` itself queued via `InvokeAsync`. That render evaluates `Disabled="@(_pageLoadInFlight || _cursorHistory.Count == 0)"` while a pool thread pushes onto the same non-thread-safe `Stack<T>` — and can show page-2 rows with Previous still disabled. `TenantDetailPage.razor:615-617` fixed and documented this exact hazard ("a superseded member page could overwrite newer rows and mutate `_memberCursorHistory` while the renderer was reading its Count for CanGoPrevious"), and `GlobalAdministratorsPage.LoadAsync:889-897` marshals its own `Loading` write for the same reason; the cursor writes in the same methods were left out. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1081-1082]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Marshal and generation-guard the audit page's `OnParametersSetAsync` state writes — the tenant-change branch awaits `previousLease.DisposeAsync().ConfigureAwait(false)`, a remote unsubscribe that genuinely suspends, and then runs `ClearPaging(); _tenantDetailSnapshot = null; _globalAdministratorsSnapshot = null; _snapshot = TenantAuditSnapshot.Loading(tenantId);` on the resumed thread. `ComponentBase` has already called `StateHasChanged()` for the incomplete task, so a render is in flight: `ClearPaging():744-753` calls `_selectedCorrectionRoles.Clear()` on a plain `Dictionary` that render reads via `TryGetValue` at `:857`, which can throw `InvalidOperationException` and tear down the circuit. The separate `Loading` write at `:382` escapes both the dispatcher and the `CanApply` check that every other `_snapshot` write in the same method carries (`:392`), so a manual Refresh or filter change interleaving between `BeginLoad()` and that write overwrites the newer load's applied page with `Loading`. The same file marshals every other cross-thread write and comments why (`:437-446`, `:1222`, `:1267-1276`); the GA sibling marshals **and** generation-guards its equivalent `Loading` write (`GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:865-876`). Found independently by two layers. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantAuditPage.razor:319-341]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Marshal the detail page's first-load result application — after `await detailTask.ConfigureAwait(false)` / `await membersTask.ConfigureAwait(false)`, `OnParametersSetAsync` writes `_snapshot` and `_tenantUsersSnapshot` directly at `:347-348`, writes `_memberCursor = null; _memberCursorHistory.Clear();` at `:303-304` after a `ConfigureAwait(false)` lease dispose, and writes `_readRefreshLease`/`_subscriptionTenantId` at `:385-386` the same way — all on thread-pool threads while Blazor's incomplete-task render runs. Every other write path in the same file marshals through `InvokeAsync` with a re-check inside and a comment explaining that the unmarshalled version was a defect (`RefreshTenantReadsAsync:493-512`, `LoadMemberPageAsync:592-616` and `:620-650`); `OnParametersSetAsync` was left on the old shape. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:300-347]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Re-check the load generation *inside* the dispatcher callback on the GA and audit pages — both call `CanApply(generation)` on the thread-pool continuation and then `await InvokeAsync(() => { _snapshot = result; … })` with no re-check, while the `Loading` write 20 lines earlier in the GA page does re-check inside `InvokeAsync` with a comment explaining why. `TenantDetailPage.razor:624-629` re-checks inside the callback and states the reason ("a superseded member page could overwrite newer rows"). Concretely: a notification refresh that passes `CanApply` and is then overtaken by `ResetPagingAsync` (which clears cursor state synchronously at `:934-935`) lands page-N rows on top of the newer page-one snapshot while `_currentCursor` is `null`, so the next Next pushes `null` into history. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:895-917]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] Pin the two new global-administrator support-safe `ToString()` overrides — `grep -rn "GlobalAdministratorsSnapshot\b.*ToString\|GlobalAdministratorsRequest.*ToString" tests/` returns nothing, and neither override is invoked in any test. Deleting either restores the compiler-generated record `ToString`, which prints `Rows` (every administrator user id), `NextCursor` (the protected cursor), `ETag`, `ProjectionVersion` and `RequestCursor` — and no test fails. Every sibling override is pinned by an exact-format assertion: `TenantListSnapshot` (`TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:5085`), `TenantDetailSnapshot` (`:1098`, `:1177`, `:1250`), audit and user-membership (`Audit_and_user_membership_snapshot_diagnostics_omit_protected_state`), `TenantUsersSnapshot`/`TenantUsersRequest` (`TenantUsersSnapshotTests.cs:85` and `Diagnostics_omit_literal_scope_cursor_etag_and_projection_version`). Violates AC6 and the **Never** clause on cursors/ETags/payloads in diagnostics. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/State/GlobalAdministrators/GlobalAdministratorsSnapshot.cs:177]

Inserting and removing a child mutates the ancestor region, so each projection nudge re-announces the whole state block assertively and interrupts the screen-reader user — while the story requires the nudge to be a polite, non-authoritative signal. Same shape on the audit page. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:100-121]

The retention property its comment describes is delivered entirely by `PendingOperation.Dispose()` calling `CompleteCancellation`, which would work identically with the list removed. The lock is load-bearing; the list is not. Present in all three pages. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:190-199]

[src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:772-775]

Deleting it keeps 1,565/1,565 green. The `[InlineData("%2F%2Fevil.test%2Ftenants")]` row at `TenantDetailSurfaceTests.cs:439` reads as proof the guard works, but that input is rejected by the prefix clause alone — the risk is reading a passing test as evidence a security-adjacent rule is exercised. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/TenantDetailPage.razor:863-866]

#### Deferred

- [x] [Review][Defer] The authentication-change path re-authorizes from raw claims, bypassing the principal resolver [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:1279] — deferred to Story 1.11 (owner decision, 2026-07-30). Reason: 1.11 already owns both open principal-resolution decisions on this evaluator, so fixing the seam here would mean guessing how they resolve; the chunk A+B review folded the structurally identical `TenantDetailPage` item there for the same reason.
- [x] [Review][Defer] The member evidence gate collapses lifecycle and permission reasons into "stale data" [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:496-499] — deferred; the `!ActionsAreEvidenceBacked -> StaleData` check was inserted by this change above the pre-existing `Detail.Status is Disabled or Unknown -> MissingLifecycleSupport` and `role is TenantRole.Unknown -> MissingPermission` arms, so a disabled tenant or an unknown-role member now reports "stale data" whenever any evidence clause is short. Defensible as written: without current, version-consistent evidence the code genuinely cannot assert a lifecycle or permission conclusion, so failing to the weakest claim is the fail-closed reading. Kept as a recorded design choice rather than a defect; revisit if operators report the reason as unhelpful.
- [x] [Review][Defer] Mobile read-only for platform-authority mutations is enforced only by CSS [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor.css:200-216] — deferred; `@media (max-width: 42rem)` sets `display: none` on `.global-admins__mutation-initiation` while the `EditForm`, the grant submit button and the per-row Remove `FluentButton` stay rendered and wired over the circuit, and `SubmitGrantAsync`/`PreviewRemove`/`SubmitRemoveAsync` contain no viewport check. Viewport is an affordance, not an authorization boundary — the server API and the existing authorization, read-surface and freshness gates remain the real enforcement — so this is a copy-accuracy point ("requires a wider viewport") rather than a security defect. Revisit if the notice is ever restated as a safety guarantee.

#### Repair record — chunk C+D+E (2026-07-30)

All 38 patch items above were applied, plus the two patches derived from the resolved decisions. Story 1.11
inherited the third decision as a recorded deferral.

Verification at completion:

- `dotnet build Hexalith.Tenants.slnx -c Release` — **0 warnings, 0 errors**.
- `Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests` — **1,600 passed** (was 1,565; +35 tests), 0 failed, 0 skipped.
- `Hexalith.Tenants.Server.Tests` 738, `Hexalith.Tenants.Client.Tests` 50,
  `Hexalith.Tenants.Contracts.Tests` 120, `Hexalith.Tenants.Testing.Tests` 181 — all passing.
- `python3 scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py` — exit 0, six declared pointers.
- Every mutation the verification-gap layer reported as surviving was re-run against the repaired suite and
  now **fails**, except the one noted below.

Two things a later reviewer should not have to rediscover:

1. **`TenantAuditPage.NextPageAsync`'s tenant-identity clause remains unpinned, by construction.** After a
   route change `LoadAsync`'s own `CanApply` already refuses to publish the late result, so
   `ReferenceEquals(_snapshot, result)` rejects it first and removing the identity clause changes no
   observable behaviour. The clause is kept as defence in depth and annotated in place; the behaviour the
   three clauses jointly provide is pinned by
   `A_next_page_completing_after_the_route_moved_does_not_commit_its_cursor_onto_the_new_tenant`. This is
   recorded rather than papered over with a test that would assert something else and read as coverage.
2. **Two pre-existing assertions were changed, not just added.** `Detail_page_loads_through_gateway_...` and
   `Detail_page_composes_member_access_review_...` asserted `ShouldContain("2 members")`, a prefix both
   member-summary variants share. Tightening them revealed that both fixtures build the detail snapshot with
   the default `Unknown` lifecycle and no projection version, so they had been passing on the
   *evidence-unavailable* branch while their names imply a healthy overview. They now assert that branch
   explicitly; both branches are covered by the new theory. Likewise
   `Completed_grant_without_projection_evidence_...` built a complete page (null cursor, `HasMore: false`)
   without setting `IsCompleteEvidence`, which the gateway would compute as `true` for that shape — the
   fixture was corrected so the test still exercises the genuine "did not confirm" path it names.

Incidental to the patches: the first-page-sentinel trim was extracted to `State/CursorHistory.cs` rather
than triplicated across the three pagers, and `MemberAccessReview.RequestNextPageAsync` /
`RequestPreviousPageAsync` were widened from `private` to `internal` so their pre-render click guards are
reachable from the test project, which already has `InternalsVisibleTo`.

Still outstanding on this story, unchanged by this pass: the 25 open patch items from the chunk A+B review
(2026-07-30), and chunk F (integration tests, Aspire topology, route smoke, evidence/provenance docs), which
no review pass has covered.

#### Repair record — chunk A+B (2026-07-30)

All 25 open patch items from the chunk A+B review were applied, and the deferred `EventStore:BaseAddress`
compound-scheme item was closed as a consequence rather than left inconsistent with the read side.

Verification at completion:

- `dotnet build Hexalith.Tenants.slnx -c Release` — **0 warnings, 0 errors**.
- `Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests` **1,611 passed** (1,565 at the start of this review loop; +46), plus
  `Server.Tests` 738, `Client.Tests` 50, `Contracts.Tests` 120, `Testing.Tests` 181 — all passing, 0 failed.
- Every mutation used to justify one of these items was re-run against the repaired suite and now fails.

The changes a later reviewer should know about, because they alter a shipped contract rather than only
adding a guard:

1. **Aspire service discovery is gone from the read client**, per the owner's resolved option (c), and the
   `Microsoft.Extensions.ServiceDiscovery` package reference with it. The scheme gate is now plain
   `http`/`https` for BOTH `Tenants:BaseAddress` and `EventStore:BaseAddress`, and a well-formed compound
   scheme (`https+http://…`) throws at composition instead of silently resolving the unavailable gateway —
   an operator cannot tell that silent form apart from an outage. A malformed value such as `http++https`
   is deliberately NOT treated as a compound scheme: it is a typo and keeps the existing fail-closed
   behaviour, so this guard reports only the one misconfiguration it can describe accurately. The AppHost
   already injects `tenantsApi.GetEndpoint("https")`, a resolved URL, so the live topology is unaffected.
2. **All-dot and separator route identifiers are now rejected, not escaped.** `%2E` escaping was defence
   against a value that resolves to a path segment, but it is not durable — the upstream API may normalize
   `%2E` during routing, and a resolved `..` under a future DAPR invoke path could traverse out of the
   `/v1.0/invoke/{appId}/method/` prefix. An unusable route identity now maps to `InvalidRequest`/400
   rather than `Unavailable`/503, so a caller-side input defect stays distinguishable from the API being
   down. `Dot_only_route_identifiers_are_percent_encoded_…` was replaced by
   `Dot_only_and_separator_route_identifiers_are_rejected_without_sending_a_request`.
3. **`TenantsRestQueryResponse.Freshness` was removed.** Nothing outside the record ever read it; the
   rendered freshness comes from `TenantQueryGateway.ResolveFreshness`, a second and stricter implementation
   that normalizes through `ProjectionLifecyclePolicy`. Publishing a competing value made the client's tests
   read as if they pinned the rendered banners. The client still resolves freshness internally, where it is
   load-bearing — as the input to the supported-304 gate — and that use is now pinned explicitly.
4. **`EnrichRowsAsync` is bounded.** It previously issued up to 100 strictly sequential detail reads per
   list render against a client whose only configured option is `BaseAddress`, so `HttpClient.Timeout` was
   the 100 s default: one slow API could block a single render for ~100 × 100 s, retriggered by every
   projection notification. It now runs at `MaximumHydrationConcurrency` under a 10 s whole-page deadline,
   verifies the returned tenant identity, and contains every per-row failure — a supplementary member-count
   read can no longer collapse a confirmed page.
5. **The GA `304` version promotion is bound to `Current`**, and `ResolveNotModifiedLifecycle` no longer
   inherits a lifecycle the response never asserted. Both fed `IsCompleteEvidence`, which is what makes
   "this user is not a global administrator" conclusive.

Two test-harness corrections worth naming, since both had been reading as coverage:

- **`GlobalAdministratorsPageTests.StubTenantsBffComposition` never overrode the async authorization
  resolver**, so every test there hit the default interface implementation — the old synchronous claims
  property — and the page's fail-closed `catch` was unreachable because that default cannot throw. The stub
  now returns `Indeterminate` from the synchronous property and the real value only from the async resolver;
  reverting the page to claims-only authorization fails **55 tests**.
- **113 ETag literals in the gateway tests were quoted** (`"\"known\""`), a value that can never round-trip:
  `GetStrongETag` strips quotes and `NormalizeValidator` rejects any ETag containing one, so against the
  real client those `304` paths were unreachable. They now model what the transport actually produces.
- The `RestQueryClientAdapter` seam is retained as a response-driving harness and documented as such; the
  17 assertions that pinned its own hardcoded aggregate ids, query types and projection types — which could
  not fail for any production change — were removed. Assertions on values the gateway actually supplied
  (tenant ids, cursors, page sizes, ETags, call counts) were kept.
