### Review Findings — review loop 8, chunk F + production `src/` delta (2026-07-31)

Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) over two ranges:
**chunk F** = `8d64563..625061b` limited to `src/Hexalith.Tenants.AppHost`,
`tests/Hexalith.Tenants.IntegrationTests`, `tests/test-summary.md` (never reviewed by any prior pass), and
**chunk G-src** = `d377e33..625061b` limited to `src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI` (the loop-7 repairs plus the
undeclared `33abe27` badge feature and `a49d793` cursor-history work). 3,625 diff lines / 40 files.
The UI unit-test delta (`d377e33..HEAD -- tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests`, ~1,740 added lines) was read by
the verification-gap layer but is otherwise **not** reviewed and remains outstanding.

59 raw findings deduped to 43, of which 2 were dismissed after verification, leaving 41 tracked (4
decisions, 33 patches, 4 deferrals). The verification-gap layer ran 46 production mutations in the full
1,654-test UI suite: 21 killed, 22 survived, 3 uncompilable; every mutation reverted and the tree verified
byte-identical. Gitlink guard re-run: exit 0 — but see decision item 4, the guard does not compare values.

Severities below are this workflow's, assigned after reading the code at each site; three layer-assigned
`high` ratings were lowered because the scenario is unreachable against the current producer or consumer set.

#### Decisions needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] Lifecycle gate contradicts recorded decision D6 and hides the accurate reason — `33abe27` changed `TenantLifecycleAvailabilityInput.Evaluate` from `Lifecycle is not Unknown and not Current` to `Lifecycle is not Current`, and added four new `Lifecycle is not ProjectionLifecycleState.Current` gates ahead of the existing `ProjectionState`/`Freshness` clauses. Decision D6 (2026-07-28) resolved that `IsStale: false` with an absent lifecycle header IS legitimate projection-backed `Current` evidence, and `ResolveFreshness` still implements exactly that. The two now disagree: that wire shape yields `Freshness = Current` + `Lifecycle = Unknown`, which renders a "Current" badge over a surface where every configuration, metadata and enable/disable command is blocked. **Not reachable against today's producer** — all six handlers go through `ToQueryResponseMetadata`, which couples `Lifecycle = Unknown` to `IsStale = null`, so freshness is `Unknown` too and the next clause blocks anyway — so the availability outcome is unchanged. What IS live today is the message regression: because `TenantDetailSnapshot.Unavailable`/`Degraded`/`Stale` all carry a non-`Current` lifecycle, the clause ordering makes `Tenants.Configuration.Set.Unavailable.ProjectionState`, `...Unavailable.Freshness` and `Tenants.Lifecycle.Unavailable.StaleFreshness` unreachable for those flows, and an operator whose read simply failed is told to refresh the projection lifecycle. Options: (a) reverse D6 and keep the strict gate, moving the lifecycle clause after the ProjectionState/Freshness clauses so the accurate reason still wins; (b) uphold D6, restore `and not Unknown` on all five sites, and let the freshness clause do the blocking; (c) uphold D6 in `ResolveFreshness` but record the strict gate as an intentional narrowing with its own amendment entry. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/State/TenantDetail/TenantLifecycleAvailability.cs:44]  **RESOLVED by decision D-F (loop 10) and APPLIED (loop 10).** D6 is reversed, the strict gate stands at all five sites, and the lifecycle clause is evaluated after the ProjectionState/Freshness clauses so the accurate reason wins. Checked here because the item was still open against Story 1.10's list.
- [x] [Review][Decision] A 39-file feature commit landed inside the story range declared by no story — `33abe27` "feat(ui): add projection lifecycle badges" introduces `ProjectionLifecycleBadge.razor` and `ProjectionLifecycleStatus.razor`, rewrites `TruthStateBadge`, adds lifecycle gates to four command flows, and renames the shipped EN/FR string `Tenants.List.Column.Freshness` from "Truth state" to "Freshness". Neither `spec-1-10` nor `spec-1-11` mentions `ProjectionLifecycleBadge` (0 hits in both). It is not covered by any Story 1.10 acceptance criterion, and it falsifies the File List boundary statement at `spec:405-413` claiming `TenantsWorkspace.razor`'s entire in-range net change is Story 1.11's global-administrator entry. Options: (a) attribute it to Story 1.10 and add the files plus an AC amendment; (b) split it forward to its own story key as was done for 1.11, since it is published on `main`; (c) revert the behavioural parts and keep only the presentational badge.  **RESOLVED by decision D4 (loop 9) and D-E (loop 10).** The badge work is split forward to Story 1.12, whose spec now exists with a full File List.
- [x] [Review][Decision] No test exercises the six direct routes against the running topology — `AspireTopologyTests` injects `AuditRestQueryClientAdapter`, a test-local `ITenantsRestQueryClient` that re-implements the pre-1.10 generic `SubmitQueryAsync` path and throws `NotSupportedException` for the other five reads; its `ConvertAsync` stamps `TenantsRestQueryFailureKind.None` unconditionally, so a live 503 reaches the gateway as a success with a null payload. The compensating `Direct_rest_client_routes_match_the_generated_controllers_and_parse_their_real_headers` runs in-process against `WebApplicationFactory`, and the only end-to-end `Tenants__BaseAddress` checks live in `TenantsUiRouteSmokeTests`, which is Tier 3 `continue-on-error` and self-skips. AC1 and AC7 both call for live proof. Options: (a) drive the live test through the real `TenantsRestQueryClient` against the Aspire endpoint; (b) accept the in-process cross-check as sufficient and record the live gap as an owned platform limitation; (c) promote the route smoke lane out of `continue-on-error`. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.IntegrationTests/AspireTopologyTests.cs:421]  **RESOLVED (loop 11, owner decision): option (b).** The six routes are proven in process against the real generated controllers -- routes, query strings, the projection-lifecycle header and the conditional 304 path, all extended by this loop. A live socket probe against the `tenants-api` Aspire resource was written and every read times out at the client's 60 s bound in the local slim-mode topology, which also intermittently fails the pre-existing command-status wait; a lane that cannot separate "the routes are broken" from "the topology is unhealthy" is not evidence, so it is not shipped. Recorded as an owned limitation in `tests/test-summary.md`, in the same class as `PLAT-FRESH-1`. The item's secondary claim -- that the adapter's unconditional `FailureKind.None` lets "a live 503 reach the gateway as a success with a null payload" -- is **refuted**: `EventStoreGatewayClient` throws `EventStoreGatewayException` for every non-success status, so a failing response never reaches `ConvertAsync`; it propagates to the gateway's own exception handler, which is the production mapping. Loop 11 verified this by mapping a payload-less result to `Unavailable` instead: no live outage was newly caught, and the assertion that exists because the pre-Story-4.7 alias legitimately yields no payload broke. The change was reverted and the adapter's remark now records the refutation.
- [x] [Review][Decision] The gitlink guard cannot catch the failure it was created for — `validate-story-gitlinks.py` verifies that each moved `references/` path appears as a File List entry; it does not compare the declared target SHA against the tree. The story's pointer table therefore passes while being factually wrong (see the patch item below). Options: (a) extend the validator to compare declared target SHAs against `git ls-tree`, failing on drift; (b) leave the validator as-is and treat SHA accuracy as a manual completion step; (c) drop target SHAs from the declaration format entirely and declare paths plus provenance only.  **RESOLVED (loop 11, owner decision): option (a).** `validate-story-gitlinks.py` now compares every stated `from -> to` target SHA against the tree, so a declaration naming the right path while recording a superseded commit fails. Run against the pre-correction story it reported all three stale targets; it exits 0 against the corrected table.

#### Deferred

- [x] [Review][Defer] Composition availability-pair guard is logically asymmetric [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:60] — deferred, low reach. `gatewayIsUnavailable` compares `ImplementationType` against `UnavailableTenantQueryGateway`, which is `internal` and null for factory or instance registrations, so a truthful `IsConnected: false` pair registered any other way is rejected with an inverted message while the mismatched pairing the guard exists to catch passes; the whole check is also skipped unless availability is registered as an instance. Unreachable in practice: `Hexalith.Tenants.UI` ships as a container application, not a NuGet package, the only production caller is its own `Program.cs`, and the sole assemblies that can name the internal type are the two test projects, which register the instance form.
- [x] [Review][Defer] Member mutation flows are outside the lifecycle policy [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/RemoveTenantMemberFlow.razor:223] — deferred, pending the lifecycle decision above. `ChangeTenantMemberRoleFlow`, `RemoveTenantMemberFlow`, `AddTenantMemberFlow` and `CreateTenantFlow` have no `Lifecycle` parameter and gate on freshness and surface kind only, so the gating asymmetry with the four configuration and metadata flows is a consequence of that decision, not an independent defect.
- [x] [Review][Defer] Two global-administrator teardown paths may be untestable under bUnit [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Pages/GlobalAdministratorsPage.razor:997] — deferred, pre-existing harness limit. Un-marshalling the `ResetPagingAsync` clear and neutering the `ObjectDisposedException` catch filter both survived, but bUnit's single-threaded renderer cannot reproduce either race; record them as knowingly unverified rather than leaving the code comments to imply coverage.
- [x] [Review][Defer] Paging guards widened to `internal` for direct test access [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:585] — deferred, pre-existing test-design debt. The accompanying comment concedes every guard could be deleted with the suite still green; widening production visibility gave them coverage that still does not prove the rendered control behaves correctly.

#### Dismissed after verification

- The 10 s enrichment deadline permanently degrading a 100-tenant list to `Unknown` freshness: the scenario assumed serialized detail reads, but `MaximumHydrationConcurrency` is 8, so the same page completes in roughly an eighth of the projected time. The coverage gap for the deadline is retained as a patch item above.
- `ResolveNotModifiedLifecycle` no longer inheriting the retained lifecycle: verified correct and deliberately fail-closed. All six handlers produce metadata through `ToQueryResponseMetadata`, which couples `Lifecycle` to `IsStale`, and the generated controller appends metadata headers before returning 304, so a supported 304 carries the lifecycle whenever the platform asserts one.

### Review Findings — review loop 9, chunk D (`tests/`) (2026-07-31)

Review loop 9. Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) over
`git diff 8d64563..HEAD -- 'tests/**'` (34 files, +8,975/-389).

**Scope note:** this pass covered `tests/` **only**. Production `src/` (60 files, +6,975/-818), `_bmad-output/`
and `references/` (16 files) were not re-reviewed in this loop; loop 8's open items over those paths stand.

Roughly 60 raw findings deduped to 38. Fourteen were dropped as duplicates of loop-8 items already open —
notably the audit `PreviousPageAsync` marshalling (`spec:867`), the refresh-loop lost wakeup (`spec:861`), the
corrupt-response 503 override (`spec:862`), the page-scoped command messages (`spec:877`), the live-topology
adapter (`spec:854`), the route-smoke remarks (`spec:888`) and the `test-summary.md` drift (`spec:890`). Three
layers independently re-derived those, which corroborates them but adds no new work.

Items marked **(verified)** were re-checked against source at HEAD during triage; the remainder carry the
originating layer's evidence and its cited mutation, and should be confirmed when repaired.

#### Decisions resolved — 2026-07-31

- [x] [Review][Decision] Gateway test harness re-implements the deleted transport — **RESOLVED: option (a), replace with a real-client fake.** `CapturingGatewayClient` + `RestQueryClientAdapter` are to be replaced by a substitute of `ITenantsRestQueryClient` returning `TenantsRestQueryResponse` values directly, so every `TenantsRestQueryFailureKind` is injectable at the production seam. This retires the 23 inert `Request.Tenant`/`QueryType`/`Domain` assertions as a side effect and unblocks the two patch items below that depend on it.
- [x] [Review][Decision] Two composition tests pin all-or-nothing registration — **RESOLVED: option (a), fix production per AC2.** `EnsureSendableOrThrow` is removed from both sides; each base address falls through its own `TryGetHttpBaseAddress` gate and registers the unavailable gateway for that side only. `TryGetHttpBaseAddress` already rejects compound schemes via `IsSendableScheme`, so the throw was purely a louder duplicate that converted a per-side fail-closed case into a whole-module boot failure. Both tests are rewritten to assert per-side fail-closed, which also resolves their inconsistency with the malformed-value tests in the same class. Closes loop-8 `spec:872`.
- [x] [Review][Decision] `GlobalAdministratorsSnapshot.Error()` has no producer — **RESOLVED: option (a), give it a producer — but NOT by the mechanism named in the decision text.** That text proposed mapping `TenantsRestQueryFailureKind.Unknown` to `Error`; on verification `Unknown` is produced **zero** times by `TenantsRestQueryClient` (`MapFailure` emits only `Unauthorized`/`Forbidden`/`NotFound`/`InvalidRequest`/`Unavailable`, and no explicit `Failure(...)` call uses it), so that would have left `Error` exactly as unreachable. The mechanism actually used: stop `ToEventStoreResult` forcing `Unavailable` to `503`, so a genuine `500`/`502`/`504` keeps its real status instead of being flattened into the declared-outage code. `Timeout`, `InvalidPayload`, `InvalidMetadata` and `Unknown` stay forced to `503` — they carry no meaningful HTTP status and the forced code is what stops them re-presenting retained rows as "last confirmed remain visible". The detail and user-tenants mappers get explicit server-error arms returning the same unavailable state they return today, so their behaviour is unchanged. **Corrected by review loop 10 (2026-07-31):** this sentence originally also named the list and tenant-users mappers, which cannot carry status arms at all — `MapTenantListException` switches on authorization only and `MapTenantUsersResponse` switches on `TenantsRestQueryFailureKind`, not on status. The same loop replaced the three enumerated codes with the whole `>= 500 and < 600` range, because listing `500`/`502`/`504` left `501`, `505`, `506`, `507`, `508`, `510` and `511` falling to the `Degraded` default arm — retained-evidence semantics on a first load with nothing retained, and a regression against the pre-D3 behaviour where every 5xx collapsed to `503`; the global-administrator and audit mappers reach their `Error` defaults, which is the AC6 distinction between "the server failed unexpectedly" and "the service is down".
- [x] [Review][Decision] Seven undeclared test files — **RESOLVED: option (a), split the badge work forward and re-cut Story 1.6.** `33abe27`'s projection-lifecycle-badge work (six test files plus its `src/` files, which loop-8 `spec:853` raises separately) takes its own story key, as was done for Story 1.11, because it is already published on `main`. The configuration-flow tests are attributed to Story 1.6, whose range is re-cut to include them. Note independently of attribution: `2cd7edf` post-dates the endpoint of every prior review range, so whichever story owns it still owes it a first review pass.

- [x] [Review][Decision] The gateway's own test harness re-implements the transport this story deleted — `RestQueryClientAdapter` (`TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:5930`) builds a `SubmitQueryRequest` for all six reads and hardcodes `TenantsRestQueryFailureKind.None` at `:6060`, so the ~60 `CapturingGatewayClient`-driven tests exercise only the success arm of `ToEventStoreResult`. `grep SubmitQueryRequest src/` returns zero hits — production has no such symbol. Failures can only be injected as `EventStoreGatewayException`, which the real `ITenantsRestQueryClient` never throws. **(verified)**: `InvalidMetadata` occurs 0 times in `TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs` (3 times in `TenantsRestQueryClientTests.cs`), and the adapter also carries 13 lines of dead freshness computation at `:6044-6056` that re-implement `ResolveFreshness` and discard the result. Options: (a) replace the adapter with a substitute of the real `ITenantsRestQueryClient` returning `TenantsRestQueryResponse` values directly, so every `FailureKind` is injectable at the production seam; (b) keep the adapter for response-driving and add a parallel failure-injection seam; (c) accept the harness and move all failure-mapping coverage to `TenantsRestQueryClientTests`.  **Superseded by loop 10's reopened item** (`spec:1191`), which carries the remaining harness replacement. The misleading half -- 23 inert `Request.*` assertions and the adapter's dead freshness ladder -- is already closed.
- [x] [Review][Decision] Two composition tests pin the all-or-nothing registration AC2 forbids — `Compound_service_discovery_address_is_rejected_at_composition_time` (`TenantsUiCompositionTests.cs:251`) and `Compound_event_store_address_is_rejected_at_composition_time` (`:275`) assert `AddHexalithTenantsUiModule` **throws**, with an in-test comment "it must fail loudly at boot". `EnsureSendableOrThrow("Tenants:BaseAddress", …)` runs before the EventStore block, so a bad read address prevents command/status registration entirely — and the mirror case kills the read side. AC2 and the "Separate dependencies" matrix row require each side to fail closed on its own reference with no all-or-nothing registration. The same class simultaneously encodes the opposite contract for malformed values (`:342`, `:367`). Loop 8 records the production site (`spec:872`) but not that two tests hold it in place, so that patch cannot land without deciding this. Options: (a) fix production per AC2 and rewrite both tests to assert per-side fail-closed; (b) amend AC2 to allow fail-loud on compound addresses specifically; (c) keep both behaviours and document the split.  **RESOLVED by loop 9 decision D2**, applied in loop 10. This is the original finding text, retained above its resolution.
- [x] [Review][Decision] `GlobalAdministratorsSnapshot.Error()` has no production producer — `MapGlobalAdministratorsException`'s `_ => Error()` default is dead: **(verified)** `MapFailure` emits only `Unauthorized`/`Forbidden`/`NotFound`/`InvalidRequest`/`Unavailable`, and `ToEventStoreResult` forces `Unavailable|Timeout|InvalidPayload|InvalidMetadata|Unknown` to 503, so the synthesized status is always one of {400, 401, 403, 404, 503} — all matched by explicit arms. The `:683` 304-without-snapshot arm is equally unreachable. Yet `Get_global_administrators_maps_gateway_status_to_safe_snapshot_state` `[InlineData(500, …Error)]` and the page test for `tenants-global-admins-error` both reach it only through the harness. AC6 requires `error` to stay distinct from `unavailable`; today every 5xx and every malformed payload collapses to `Unavailable`, and the `Error` copy plus its Retry/Reset affordances ship unreachable. Options: (a) map a genuine class of failure to `Error`; (b) delete the state, its copy and its affordances; (c) keep it as a defensive default and delete the tests that claim to cover it.  **RESOLVED by loop 9 decision D3**, applied in loop 10 and corrected by it. This is the original finding text, retained above its resolution.
- [x] [Review][Decision] Seven changed test files are declared by no story, and `2cd7edf` post-dates every review pass — undeclared against both File Lists: `EditTenantMetadataFlowTests.cs`, `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlowTests.cs`, `SetTenantConfigurationFlowTests.cs`, `TenantLifecycleActionAvailabilityTests.cs`, `TenantListSurfaceTests.cs`, `TruthStateBadgeTests.cs`, `State/TenantLifecycleAvailabilityTests.cs`. Story 1.6 declares two of them but for range `2f190a1..ec7ec8c`, and `ec7ec8c` is not an ancestor of HEAD in this range. Six come from `33abe27` (covered only as a whole-commit decision at `spec:853`, which enumerates `src/` files). `SetTenantConfigurationFlowTests.cs` also changed in `2cd7edf`, which is **after** `625061b` — the endpoint of loop 8's ranges — so nothing has reviewed it; it removed the public `IsAuthorized` parameter from `SetTenantConfigurationFlow.razor` and added reauthorization-provider tests that no 1.10 AC covers. Options: (a) attribute to 1.10 with AC amendments; (b) split forward to a new story key as was done for 1.11; (c) attribute the configuration-flow work to 1.6 and re-cut its range.  **RESOLVED by loop 9 decision D4** and loop 10 decision D-E. This is the original finding text, retained above its resolution.

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] Pin `Uri.EscapeDataString` on route identifiers — **(verified)** the only escaping test uses `TenantId = "tenant.alpha"` and asserts `/api/tenants/tenant.alpha`; dots are unreserved, so `EscapeDataString` is the identity function there and the call is unpinned. `TryEscapeRouteValue` rejects only `/`, `\` and all-dot values, and `CreateRequestUri` runs with `DangerousDisablePathAndQueryCanonicalization = true`. Replace the escape with `escaped = value;` and the suite passes — a caller-supplied tenant id of `alpha?pageSize=9999` then splices a query parameter into the request and `alpha#x` truncates at the fragment. Tenant ids are caller-supplied strings by project rule, so this is reachable input. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClientTests.cs:599] — **APPLIED 2026-07-31**
- [x] [Review][Patch] Give the Remove configuration flow the two reauthorization fail-closed tests the Set flow gained — **(verified)** `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:478-480` is the same `ReauthorizeProvider is null ? … : await ReauthorizeProvider()` shape as the Set flow, but `Submit_fails_closed_when_no_reauthorize_provider_is_wired` and `Submit_fails_closed_when_reauthorization_throws` exist only in `SetTenantConfigurationFlowTests.cs` (`:322`, `:345`); every Remove submit test wires a provider. Change the null branch to fall back to `Context` — exactly the bug the Set tests catch — and a **destructive** `RemoveTenantConfiguration` dispatches against a possibly-revoked grant with the suite green. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Components/RemoveTenantConfigurationFlowTests.cs:180] — **APPLIED 2026-07-31**
- [x] [Review][Patch] Pin the contradiction reset's `isStale = null` — **(verified)** `Missing_non_projection_or_contradictory_metadata_fails_closed_to_unknown` builds four **200** responses and asserts only `Metadata.Lifecycle`. Delete `isStale = null` from the `malformedFreshness || contradiction` block: a 304 carrying `Lifecycle: Current` + `X-Hexalith-Is-Stale: true` then leaves `isStale = true`, `ResolveFreshness` returns `Stale` through the `Unknown` arm, and `IsSupportedNotModified` accepts `Stale` — the gateway retains the old payload on evidence the client just declared contradictory. This defeats the story's central invariant that metadata-deficient 304s never prove recovery. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantsRestQueryClientTests.cs:308] — **APPLIED 2026-07-31**
- [x] [Review][Patch] Cover the global-administrator complete-evidence lifecycle gate — `IsCompleteGlobalAdministratorsEvidence` requires `lifecycle == ProjectionLifecycleState.Current`, and `GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot` confirms a removal on `row is null && snapshot.IsCompleteEvidence` with no lifecycle check of its own. The projection-version clause has `Get_global_administrators_requires_a_non_blank_projection_version_for_complete_evidence`; the lifecycle clause has no counterpart, and the diff added two lifecycle tests to the Grant twin and none to Remove. Delete the lifecycle clause and, on a `Rebuilding` projection reporting `X-Hexalith-Is-Stale: false`, absence of a row is reported to the operator as a **confirmed removal of a global administrator**. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/State/GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshotTests.cs:128] — **APPLIED 2026-07-31**
- [x] [Review][Patch] Deliver `Unauthorized`/`Forbidden`/`NotFound` to `ToEventStoreResult` for the five reads that lack it — the kind census over `TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs` is `Unavailable` x20, `None` x7, `InvalidCursor` x4, `InvalidRequest` x3, auth kinds x2 each, `Timeout` x1, `InvalidPayload` x1, `InvalidMetadata`/`Unknown` x0. The auth kinds appear only against `GetTenantUsersAsync`, which switches on `FailureKind` and bypasses `ToEventStoreResult` entirely. Add `or Unauthorized or Forbidden or NotFound` to the 503 override and every test passes — a real 401 on the detail, list, audit or global-administrators read then renders as an outage instead of offering sign-in. [tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:742] — **APPLIED 2026-07-31** (unblocked by decision D1's first increment: `FixedFailureRestQueryClient` substitutes the real `ITenantsRestQueryClient`, so a failure now flows through `ToEventStoreResult`). Mutation-verified: folding the three auth kinds into the 503 override fails 5 new tests.
- [x] [Review][Patch] Rebuild the gateway 304 fixtures around wire states the client can actually deliver — `EnqueueDetailNotModified("known")` and its five siblings default to `lifecycle: Unknown, isStale: null`, which `IsSupportedNotModified` rejects as `InvalidMetadata`; `:2676` passes `provenance: HandlerComputed`, also rejected; `:1015` uses an ETag containing literal quote characters, which `NormalizeValidator` refuses to send and `GetStrongETag` strips. The entire 304-retention surface is proven against inputs the production client cannot produce, while the input it does produce has no gateway test. #### Dismissed after verification

- Fourteen findings that re-derive loop-8 items already open (`spec:854`, `:859`, `:861`, `:862`, `:867`, `:877`, `:878`, `:888`, `:889`, `:890`, `:891`). Three independent layers reaching the same conclusions raises confidence in those items but creates no new work; the corroborating evidence is noted in the loop-8 entries' favour rather than duplicated here.
- The claim that `NormalizePageSize`'s clamp arm is a live defect: the workspace page-size selector is a fixed `[20, 50, 100]` and every other caller passes a constant, so the out-of-range branch is unreachable from the UI. The coverage gap itself remains open as `spec:873`.
- EN/FR resource-key parity: verified symmetric at 1,223 keys each, with no asymmetry introduced by this range.

### Review Findings — review loop 10, never-reviewed delta (2026-07-31)

Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) over
`git diff 625061b -- src tests` — 30 files, +1,450/-249 (2,740 diff lines). This is exactly the range no
prior loop has seen: commit `2cd7edf` (which `spec:428` records as post-dating every earlier review
endpoint) plus the uncommitted loop-9 repair set.

All four layers independently confirmed the suite green at this tree: `dotnet test
tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests` = **1,696/1,696**, Release solution build 0 warnings/0 errors, EN/FR parity
1,223/1,223 keys with no one-sided key and no placeholder mismatch, and `validate-story-gitlinks.py` exit 0
(all six moved pointers declared). Every finding below is therefore behaviour the suite does not cover.

The verification-gap layer ran 20 production mutations inside the full 1,696-test suite; 13 survived, and it
verified restoration three ways — `git status --porcelain` back to the same 23 files, `git diff --stat
625061b -- src tests` back to `30 files changed, 1450 insertions(+), 249 deletions(-)`, and a regenerated
diff byte-identical to the reviewed one.

**Triage correction the layers could not reach individually:** the two `high` findings are mutually masking.
The configuration landmark unmounts both flows the instant a command raises activity (item P-1), which is
why the Remove dialog's missing dismissal path (item P-2) cannot be reached today — the dialog disappears
instead of trapping. **P-1 and P-2 must be repaired in the same change**: fixing P-1 alone converts a
silent-unmount defect into a live keyboard trap on a destructive `aria-modal` dialog.

Severities below are this workflow's, assigned after reading each site at HEAD; the layers' own ratings were
discarded per the triage rule.

#### Decisions needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-E] Story 1.12 owns two files in this range but has no spec, and one of them carries 1.10 work — **RESOLVED (2026-07-31, owner decision D-E): option (a), author the 1.12 spec and split the file.** `spec-1-12-projection-lifecycle-badges.md` must be authored with a real File List before either story closes, and this range's `TenantListSurfaceTests.cs` change — the `NotModifiedWithoutSnapshot` remark at `:1788-1796` — is declared by Story 1.10, because it is 1.10 transport work. The badge-era content of both files stays with 1.12. This applies the same principle as decision D-B's 1.11 re-cut: each story declares the work it actually carries, and a genuinely shared file is declared by both with the overlap stated. Original finding follows. `spec:418-423` re-cuts `tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/Components/TenantListSurfaceTests.cs` and `tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests/State/TenantLifecycleAvailabilityTests.cs` to Story 1.12, and `sprint-status.yaml` carries `1-12-projection-lifecycle-badges: review` with the note that its spec still needs authoring. No `spec-1-12-*.md` exists, so both files are currently declared by a story with no File List — the same defect class as the loop-9 "seven undeclared test files" item, one level up. Worse, `TenantListSurfaceTests.cs`'s change *in this diff* is not badge work at all: it is the `NotModifiedWithoutSnapshot` remark (`TenantListSurfaceTests.cs:1788-1796`) that closes 1.10's own `spec:991`. Options: (a) author `spec-1-12-*.md` with a File List before either story closes; (b) keep the badge-era content in 1.12 but declare this range's `TenantListSurfaceTests.cs` change in 1.10, since it is 1.10 transport work; (c) revert the 1.12 re-cut for these two files and keep them in 1.10. - [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-F] A fifth lifecycle gate was added while the decision governing lifecycle gating is still open — **RESOLVED (2026-07-31, owner decision D-F): option (a) of `spec:869`, reverse D6 and reorder the clauses.** The strict `Lifecycle is not ProjectionLifecycleState.Current` gate is upheld at all five sites, including the new proof-gate site at `TenantQueryGateway.cs:2028`, and decision D6 is reversed: `IsStale: false` with an absent lifecycle header is no longer treated as sufficient projection-backed `Current` evidence for mutation gating. In exchange, the lifecycle clause moves **after** the `ProjectionState`/`Freshness` clauses at every site, so `Tenants.Configuration.Set.Unavailable.ProjectionState`, `...Unavailable.Freshness` and `Tenants.Lifecycle.Unavailable.StaleFreshness` become reachable again and an operator whose read simply failed is no longer told to refresh the projection lifecycle. This closes loop-8 item `spec:869` and keeps the new `[InlineData(ProjectionLifecycleState.Unknown)]` row correct. `ResolveFreshness`'s display classification is a separate concern and is addressed by the reordering, not by widening the gate. Original finding follows. `TenantQueryGateway.cs:2028` now requires `ResolveLifecycle(result.Metadata) is ProjectionLifecycleState.Current` before `SetConfirmed`/`RemoveConfirmed`. Loop-8 item `spec:869` ("Lifecycle gate contradicts recorded decision D6 and hides the accurate reason") is still unchecked and lists *reversing D6* among its options; this diff extends the strict gate to a fifth site before that call is made. The new theory `Configuration_projection_proof_fails_closed_when_the_projection_lifecycle_is_not_current` (`TenantQueryGatewayTests.cs:1838-1841`) includes `[InlineData(ProjectionLifecycleState.Unknown)]`, pinning exactly the wire shape D6 resolved as legitimate `Current` evidence — so the test now encodes the opposite of the recorded decision. Resolve `spec:869` first; this site follows whichever way it goes. Note also (edge-case layer, verified) that against the owned producer the clause is currently redundant: `ResolveFreshness` already rejects everything it would, and the accompanying test builds `ProjectionBackedMetadata(isStale: false, lifecycle: Rebuilding)` directly on `CapturingGatewayClient`, a pairing `ReadMetadata` resets and the wire cannot deliver. Defence-in-depth is defensible; the claim that it closes a live hole is not. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:2028]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-G] Removing `EnsureSendableOrThrow` left the per-side fail-closed path with no diagnostic on any channel — **RESOLVED (2026-07-31, owner decision D-G): option (a), inject `ILogger` into composition and log the setting name only.** D2's per-side fail-closed registration stands unchanged; what is restored is the signal, on the startup log channel. `AddHexalithTenantsUiModule` logs one support-safe line naming the configuration key (`Tenants:BaseAddress` / `EventStore:BaseAddress`) when `TryGetHttpBaseAddress` rejects it, and **never** the configured value, which can carry host, path or credential material. The clients keep `.RemoveAllLoggers()`: this is a deliberate, composition-scoped exception to that rule, not a reversal of it, and it must use the source-generated `LoggerMessageAttribute` pattern. No rendered surface, resource string or availability contract changes. Original finding follows. decision D2 correctly made rejection per-side (AC2 forbids all-or-nothing registration), but it deleted the only signal an operator ever got. `AddHexalithTenantsUiModule` takes no `ILogger`, and both typed clients call `.RemoveAllLoggers()` deliberately (`TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:103,153`); `TenantsReadSurfaceAvailability` carries only `IsConnected: false` with no reason. So `Tenants:BaseAddress=https+http://tenants` — the Aspire form, a plausible copy-paste — boots clean and renders every read as the same "read surface unavailable" state a genuine API outage produces, with nothing anywhere naming the setting. That is the "misconfiguration indistinguishable from an outage" the deleted guard's own surviving remark still describes. The *channel* is the owner call, because this module suppresses HTTP logging on purpose: (a) inject `ILogger` into composition and log the setting **name** only, never the value; (b) add a support-safe reason code to `TenantsReadSurfaceAvailability` and surface it in the unavailable state; (c) accept silent fail-closed and record it as intentional. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Extensions/TenantsUiServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:94]

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-1] Stop the configuration landmark from unmounting both flows the instant a command starts — verified end to end at HEAD: `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:502` raises `SetCommandActivityRaisedAsync(true)` *before* the submit, which reaches `TenantDetailPage.SetCommandActivityAsync` (`:926-930`) → `_commandInFlight = true` → `IsCommandSurfaceAvailable` false (`:268-269`) → fed back at `:216` → `TenantConfigurationManagement.UnavailableReason` returns the CommandSurface string (`:175-176`) → the whole `else` branch holding both flows (`:38-109`) stops rendering. Confirmed/Rejected/UnableToVerify, the safe message and the audit entry point are all lost at the moment of submit, violating "Command flows must confirm from projection evidence before showing success" and the non-collapse truth-state rule. Both flows already carry the exception the landmark lacks — `(!IsCommandSurfaceAvailable && !IsOwnedCommandInFlight)` at `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:241` and `SetTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:253` — so the intended rule is already written down one level lower. The landmark must mirror it: it already learns of remove activity through `HandleRemoveCommandActivityAsync` (`:273-281`) and needs the same signal from the Set flow, which currently passes `OnCommandActivityChanged` straight through (`:47`). **The clause and branch structure are byte-identical at `625061b`, so the defect is pre-existing — but this range's entire `_removeCommandStarted` mechanism ("once a remove command has been submitted the flow owns its own dismissal", `:186-188`) is inert in the composed page until it is fixed, which is why it is a patch here rather than a deferral.** Unproven because `Management_keeps_an_open_remove_flow_mounted_after_its_target_row_leaves_the_context` (`TenantDetailSurfaceTests.cs:653`) renders the landmark standalone and never re-renders it with `IsCommandSurfaceAvailable: false`. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Configuration/TenantConfigurationManagement.razor:175]  **APPLIED (loop 10, 2026-07-31).**
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-2] Give the Remove dialog a dismissal path in its expected post-submit state — **fix together with P-1; alone, P-1's fix makes this live.** After a successful submit the snapshot is `Accepted` or `ProjectionPending`, both of which make `IsOwnedCommandInFlight` true (`TenantCommandFlowGuard.cs:5-9`). This range disabled Cancel on that condition (`RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:120`) *and* added an early return to `CloseAsync` (`:639-642`), so the Escape handler (`:673-679`) becomes a silent no-op — no focus change, no announcement — inside a `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` section whose two `@onfocus` sentinels (`:15-18`, `:169-172`) bounce focus back in. `ProjectionPending` is not terminal and is not self-healing: `ApplyStatus(Completed)` sets it (`TenantCreateCommandModels.cs:1202-1207`) and the only exit is `ConfirmProjection` with a `RemoveConfirmed` proof (`:1268-1278`), which `GetConfigurationProjectionProofAsync` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:1993-2045`) declines whenever authorization, provenance, freshness *or* the newly added lifecycle clause fails. Projection lag is normal on at-least-once unordered pub/sub, so the dialog is undismissable for the whole lag window and permanently if the proof never lands. The sibling `SetTenantConfigurationFlow` has no such guard on `CloseEditorAsync` (`:470-476`) and never disables Cancel (`:128-132`) — the destructive flow is the only one that can trap. Keep Cancel enabled (or add a distinct dismiss affordance) while retaining the flow's mounted state, so dismissal never collapses the truth state. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Configuration/RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:639]  **APPLIED (loop 10, 2026-07-31).** **CORRECTED (loop 12, 2026-08-01):** only the first half shipped. Cancel and Escape work, but dismissal unmounts the flow rather than retaining its mounted state: `CloseAsync` releases the activity lease and the parent's `CloseRemoveFlowAsync` clears `_removeKey`, discarding the `Accepted`/`ProjectionPending` receipt and audit entry point. The in-code comment re-justifies that deliberately — an operator who dismisses has chosen to stop watching, which renders no success and confirms nothing — so it is a reinterpretation, not an oversight, and it is recorded here rather than left contradicting the item. It is also what opened loop-12 item P-C: the release had no guard against an in-flight `RefreshStatusAsync` re-raising the lease from the unmounted component.
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-3] Normalize the whole 5xx range in the detail and user-tenants mappers, not three literals — corroborated independently by three layers and verified at HEAD. `ToEventStoreResult` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2466-2477`) now preserves any status in `[500,600)` for `FailureKind.Unavailable`, and `MapFailure` (`TenantsRestQueryClient.cs:592-600`) routes every status except 400/401/403/404 to `Unavailable` carrying the raw code — so 501, 505, 506, 507, 508, 510 and 511 now reach `MapDetailException`'s `_ => TenantDetailSnapshot.Degraded(...)` (`:2756`) and `MapUserTenantException`'s `_ => UserTenantMembershipSnapshot.Degraded(...)` (`:2775`), which enumerate only 500/502/504. Before this range every 5xx collapsed to 503 → `Unavailable`, so this is a new regression on the first-load path (`:288`, `:636`), and it is precisely the "claiming retained degradation on a first-load failure" that `spec:66` forbids and AC6's distinctness requires. `MapGlobalAdministratorsException` (`:2791`) and `MapTenantAuditException` (`:2802`) already treat 501 as real, so the file is internally inconsistent too. Replace the three literals with `>= 500 and < 600 => Unavailable` in both mappers. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:2750]  **APPLIED (loop 10, 2026-07-31).**

### Review Findings — review loop 12, chunk A (production `src/` + `scripts/`) (2026-08-01)

Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) over the production half of
the never-reviewed delta: `git diff HEAD -- src scripts` plus the two untracked new files, 22 files,
+709/-155 tracked (1,930 diff lines). This is loop 11's repair set, which no loop has seen — loop 10's range
(`git diff 625061b -- src tests`, 30 files, +1,450/-249) has since grown to 46 files, +4,982/-449.

The `tests/` half (chunk B, ~3,800 lines, 24 files) and the docs half (chunk C, ~660 lines) are **not** covered
by this pass and still owe their first review.

All four layers independently confirmed the tree green: UI suite **1,794/1,794**, Release build 0 warnings /
0 errors, EN/FR parity 1,227 keys each with no one-sided key, `validate-story-gitlinks.py` exit 0 with all six
pointers declared. The verification-gap layer ran 12 mutations (5 KILLED, 8 SURVIVED) and proved restoration
three ways — `git status --porcelain` back to 51 entries, `git diff --stat HEAD -- src scripts` back to
`20 files changed, 709 insertions(+), 155 deletions(-)`, and the suite back to 1,794.

**Triage note — one root cause, four sites.** P-1/P-2/P-3/P-4 are the same failure mode: a command-activity
lease or truth state that survives only as long as its owning component is mounted, released or destroyed by a
sibling with no view of the command in flight. Loop 10's P-1/P-2 fixed the landmark's *unmount-on-submit*; the
repair introduced a *release* path with the mirror-image defect. They should be repaired together — fixing any
one alone leaves the lease reachable through the others.

Severities are this workflow's, assigned after reading each site in the working tree; the layers' own ratings
were discarded per the triage rule.

#### Decisions needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-H] What text in a story is a binding `from -> to` gitlink claim? — **RESOLVED (2026-08-01, owner decision D-H): option (a).** `stated_targets` is scoped to the same sections `declared_paths` reads (File List / Completion Notes List), and where a path is stated more than once the **last** stated target wins — which is exactly the convention `spec:771-772` already declares in prose ("earlier pointer tables are retained as historical evidence only; the current values above are authoritative") and which the parser could not see. Prose, fenced code blocks and historical tables outside those sections stop being binding claims. Original finding follows. `stated_targets` (`scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py:138-151`) scans the **entire** document while `declared_paths` (`:110-114`) is scoped to File List / Completion Notes List. Verified live: running the new check against `1-6-read-only-tenant-configuration.md` yields `stated: {'references/Hexalith.EventStore': ['440ff4c', 'e4618d9']}`, where `440ff4c` is a historical bump that story records as absorbed and `e4618d9` is explicitly disowned at its line 511 — so a tree correctly holding `c8c7003` now returns `RESULT: FAIL / [MISSTATED]` on a story that is right. A fenced code block containing `git log references/Builds 53d53ae -> 1b1c0b0` parses as a binding claim too. Compounding it, targets are unioned per path and matched with `any()` (`:148-150`, `:280-285`), so one correct row anywhere exonerates every stale row for that path — verified: a story stating both `Builds 53d53ae -> 1b1c0b0` and `Builds 1b1c0b0 -> 9999999` passes at either tree value. This story is itself the hard case: it keeps superseded pointer tables at `spec:911` and `spec:1052` and states at `spec:771-772` that "earlier pointer tables are retained as historical evidence only; the current values above are authoritative" — a convention the parser cannot see. Options: (a) scope `stated_targets` to the same sections as `declared_paths` and treat the **last** stated target per path as authoritative; (b) require an explicit marker (e.g. a `<!-- gitlink:authoritative -->` fence) and ignore every unmarked line; (c) keep the whole-document scan and require stories to delete superseded tables rather than retain them as history. [scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py:138]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-I] Should every HTTP `400` on the tenant detail read assert non-existence? — **RESOLVED (2026-08-01, owner decision D-I): option (b).** The two causes are separated. A route identity this client rejects locally (`TryEscapeRouteValue`, before any request is sent) keeps `NotFound`, because a string that cannot name a route can never name a tenant. A server-issued `400` maps to the unavailable/degraded family instead, because "the server rejected this request" is not evidence of non-existence and must not be rendered as one. This narrows, and does not reverse, what loop 11 applied at `spec:1055`/`spec:1075`. Original finding follows. `MapDetailException`'s `(int)HttpStatusCode.BadRequest => TenantDetailSnapshot.NotFound(tenantId)` (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2818`) is justified for a route identity `TryEscapeRouteValue` rejects, but `MapFailure` routes *any* server-side 400 to the same arm — a rejected header, an unsupported query parameter, a server-side validation change. On a caller-supplied string id, "this id can never name a tenant" and "the server rejected this request" are different conclusions, and the operator is told a tenant that exists does not. Loop 11 applied this deliberately (`spec:1055`/`spec:1075`), so reversing it needs the owner's call. Options: (a) keep 400 → `NotFound` and record why; (b) split the client-side escape failure (already local, before any request) from the server 400, mapping the latter to `Unavailable`/degraded; (c) add a distinct support-safe "request rejected" state. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:2818]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-J] A configuration key written outside the UI with an untypeable character is still permanently unremovable — **RESOLVED (2026-08-01, owner decision D-J): option (a).** The guard's scope is accepted as written: it prevents *this UI* from creating such a key, which is the only producer this story owns. What is corrected is the claim — the comment currently asserts it prevents "the permanently unremovable key this guard exists to prevent", which is false for keys written through `POST /api/v1/commands` or any other consumer. The comment must state the real scope, and the residual exposure is recorded rather than implied closed. Original finding follows. `ContainsUntypeableCharacter` (`SetTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:430-448`, consumed at `:751`) blocks only *this* flow's own submissions, but `project-context.md:74` records configuration keys as consumer-owned and writable through `POST /api/v1/commands` or any other producer. `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:495` requires `string.Equals(_confirmationText, TargetKey, StringComparison.Ordinal)` against typed text with no alternative confirmation, so a pre-existing key containing U+200B renders identically to its clean twin and can never be removed through the UI — the exact outcome the guard's own comment claims to prevent. Options: (a) accept it and record the scope limit honestly in the guard's comment; (b) give the remove flow a detection path and a distinct non-typed confirmation affordance for such keys; (c) normalize/compare by rune-folded form in the remove confirmation. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Configuration/SetTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:430]
- [x] [Review][Decision] [medium] [D-K] `validate-story-gitlinks.py` has no automated test and no CI wiring — **RESOLVED (2026-08-01, owner decision D-K): option (c), DEFERRED as an owned limitation.** Manual verification is accepted; introducing Python test infrastructure to a .NET repository is not warranted for this guard, and porting it to C# would duplicate the script rather than test it. The limitation is recorded in `deferred-work.md` so it is not mistaken for coverage: the script is release-gating per `project-context.md:149` and its own correctness rests on the operator running it and reading the output. Original finding follows. mutation-verified: replacing `stated = stated_targets(story_text)` with `stated = {}` (`:264`) turned a spec whose stated target was corrupted to `deadbee` from FAIL/exit 1 into **PASS/exit 0**, and the real spec still exits 0, so the normal workflow invocation stays green and nothing notices. The repo has no Python test infrastructure (no `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, or `test_*.py`) and `grep -rn validate-story-gitlinks .github/` returns nothing — the only verification is a one-time manual run recorded in prose at `tests/test-summary.md:754`. `project-context.md:149` makes this script a release-gating rule, so it is guarding the process with no guard of its own. Options: (a) add a minimal `test_validate_story_gitlinks.py` plus a CI step (introduces Python test infra to a .NET repo); (b) port the check to a C# test inside an existing test project; (c) accept manual verification and record it as an owned limitation. [scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py:264]

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-A] A transient member-read outage destroys an in-flight member command flow and permanently leaks the command-activity lease — `HasTerminalMemberRead` (`MemberAccessReview.razor:609-614`) folds the two *operational* kinds `Unavailable` and `Error` in with the three denial kinds, and `OnParametersSet` (`:627-638`) clears `_activeRemoveMemberUserId`/`_activeRemoveMemberCapture` with **no in-flight guard** — despite the clause's own comment reserving that exception ("an in-flight command legitimately keeps its flow") for Degraded/Stale/Unknown only. Sequence: submit a member removal; the follow-up read returns `Unavailable` because the Tenants API blipped; the capture that exists precisely so the flow survives its row leaving `Members.Rows` (`:376-387`) is cleared, `ActiveRemoveMember` resolves null, and the flow at `:237` unmounts. The operator never sees Confirmed / Rejected / UnableToVerify, the safe message, or the audit entry point for a command that really was submitted — a direct non-collapse violation. Worse, no member flow implements `IDisposable`/`IAsyncDisposable`, so `OnCommandActivityChanged(false)` is never sent: `TenantDetailPage._commandInFlight` stays `true` and `IsCommandSurfaceAvailable` (`:274`) is false for the life of the circuit, disabling every command surface on the page. Pre-change this was unreachable on page one (terminal snapshots come from `EmptyState` → `RequestCursor == null`, matching `_renderedMemberCursor`), so the exposure is new. Fix restricts the predicate to the denial kinds — which also closes the divergence with `TenantDetailPage.IsTerminalMemberPage` (`Unauthorized, NotFound, Invalid`), two predicates for one concept — and guards the clear on `!HasOpenMemberCommandFlow`. Note the theory `Terminal_member_refresh_releases_a_captured_command_flow` (`TenantDetailSurfaceTests.cs:4136-4171`) must be re-cut with it; it currently omits `Invalid`, which mutation-survived. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Members/MemberAccessReview.razor:609]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-B] One `_childCommandInFlight` field serves two concurrently-rendered flows, so dismissing the remove dialog unmounts an in-flight Set command — the field (`TenantConfigurationManagement.razor:124`) is written by `HandleRemoveCommandActivityAsync` (`:288`), `HandleSetCommandActivityAsync` (`:305`) **and** cleared unconditionally by `CloseRemoveFlowAsync` (`:326`). Its comment asserts "only one command can be submitted at a time -- the flows guard each other", which is false for the launch path: the per-row Remove `FluentButton` (`:83-88`) carries no `Disabled` binding, and while a Set command is settling `UnavailableReason` is `null`, so the grid keeps rendering. Sequence: submit a Set configuration command → `_childCommandInFlight = true`, both flows stay mounted; click a row's Remove, then Cancel or Escape → `CloseAsync` → `OnCloseRequested` → `CloseRemoveFlowAsync` sets `_childCommandInFlight = false` **directly**, bypassing the child latch → `UnavailableReason` returns the `CommandSurface` string → the whole `else` branch (`:38-109`) stops rendering and `SetTenantConfigurationFlow` is unmounted mid-command, taking its Accepted/ProjectionPending/Confirmed/Rejected/UnableToVerify state, safe message and audit entry point with it. Its `_hasRaisedCommandActivity` lease dies with it, so the detail page's `_commandInFlight` never clears. This is the exact collapse the `_childCommandInFlight` clause was added to fix, reachable through the sibling dialog. Track the lease per flow (or have `CloseRemoveFlowAsync` clear only the remove half). [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Configuration/TenantConfigurationManagement.razor:326]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-C] `CloseAsync` releases the activity lease, then an in-flight `RefreshStatusAsync` continuation re-raises it from an unmounted component — `CloseAsync` (`RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:663-687`) now gates only on `_isSubmitting`, not `_isRefreshing`. Sequence: submit a remove; the projection never proves it, so the state stays `ProjectionPending`; click Refresh — `RefreshStatusAsync` yields at `await gateway.GetStatusAsync(...)` (`:603`); press Escape, the escape hatch this range added. `CloseAsync` calls `SetCommandActivityRaisedAsync(false)` (`:684`) and the parent unmounts the flow. The suspended continuation then reaches `UpdateCommandActivityForSnapshotAsync()` (`:620`); because the state is still `ProjectionPending`, `RetainsCommandActivity` is true, the `_hasRaisedCommandActivity` latch (`:436-445`) flips back, and `OnCommandActivityChanged(true)` fires with **no owner left to release it**. `_childCommandInFlight` and the detail page's `_commandInFlight` latch true for the life of the circuit: the Set flow renders permanently blocked on "command surface unavailable" and tenant lifecycle actions stay disabled. Gate `CloseAsync` on `_isRefreshing` too, or make the refresh continuation no-op once the lease has been released. (The `SubmitAsync` variant was refuted: its `finally` sets `_isSubmitting = false` and calls `UpdateCommandActivityForSnapshotAsync()` synchronously on the dispatcher, so no click can interleave.) [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Components/Tenants/Configuration/RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:675]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [high] [P-D] `PagingRecovered = true` is stamped onto failed page-one re-reads, and the flag preempts the branch that protects retained rows — the guard at `TenantQueryGateway.cs:412` checks only `recovered.IsSuccess`, but `MapTenantUsersResponse` can still return a failure state from a successful HTTP read: `response.Payload?.Items is null` (`:2214`) with `previous == null` yields `EmptyState(Error)` with **zero rows**, and `response.Metadata.IsDegraded` (`:2250`) yields `Degraded` with zero rows. The unconditional `with { PagingRecovered = true }` (`:425-427`) then renders the polite "review restarted at the first page" notice (`MemberAccessReview.razor:60`) over an empty failed surface. Worse, in `TenantDetailPage.razor:630` the `else if (members.PagingRecovered)` branch matches **before** `else if (!IsUsableMemberPage(members, _memberCursor) && !IsTerminalMemberPage(members))` at `:636`, so the protective `Degraded(tenantId, retainedMembers, ...)` never runs: the operator's last-confirmed rows are discarded *and* their cursor/history cleared on a read that failed — precisely what `spec:66` forbids ("Every transient failure during a refresh must retain the matching read's last-confirmed rows, ETag, paging context") and what AC6's distinctness requires. `TenantUsersSnapshot.Degraded`'s own remarks describe this exact collapse as already fixed. #### Verified clean this pass

- **AC1** — `grep -rn "SubmitQueryAsync|/api/v1/queries|QueryRouter|HandlerAwareQueryRouter|SubmitQueryRequest|tenant-index:system" src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI` returns 0 hits.
- **AC2** — `EnsureSendableOrThrow` is gone; each base address falls through its own `TryGetHttpBaseAddress` and registers only its own side's unavailable gateway (`:111-157`). No all-or-nothing path remains.
- **AC6 / the `Never` list** — the only new log channel emits a configuration key name; no ETag, cursor, payload, token or configured value reaches it.
- **Decision D-F, all five sites** — the strict `Lifecycle is not ProjectionLifecycleState.Current` gate is retained (no `and not Unknown` restored) and evaluated after the ProjectionState/Freshness clauses at exactly five sites: `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:243`, `SetTenantConfigurationFlow.razor:254`, `TenantConfigurationManagement.razor:178`, `EditTenantMetadataFlow.razor:240`, `TenantLifecycleAvailability.cs:64`. The proof gate (`TenantQueryGateway.cs:2073`) already evaluated lifecycle after freshness. All 14 EN/FR reason keys the reordering makes reachable exist in both `.resx` files.
- **Loop-11 `src`-side claims spot-verified as genuinely applied:** `spec:1053`, `spec:1054`, `spec:1055`/`spec:1075`, `spec:1059`, `spec:1060`, `spec:1061`, `spec:1063`, `spec:1064`, `spec:1066`, `spec:1067`/`spec:1068`, `spec:1069`, `spec:1076`, `spec:1079`, `spec:1167`.
- **Gitlink pointers** — the validator exits 0 against the corrected table; six declared pointers all match `git ls-tree HEAD references/`. The findings above are guard *strength*, not a live miss on this story.

#### Dismissed after verification

- Five mutations were KILLED, confirming those guards are genuinely pinned: `_childCommandInFlight`, the remove-flow `CanRefresh` fold, the remove-flow `CloseAsync` lease release, `_focusHeadingPending`, and the Management clause reorder.
- `CursorHistory.Trim`'s jump detection — the sentinel arithmetic was walked for 51 and 100+ entries; the `_pagingHistoryTruncated && _currentCursor is null && Count == 0` condition fires on exactly the pop that reaches page one, and every reset path clears both flags.
- `RemoveTenantConfigurationFlow.CloseAsync` releasing the *Set* flow's lease directly — `SetCommandActivityRaisedAsync` is latched by `_hasRaisedCommandActivity`, so the child's release is a no-op when it never submitted. The leak is at the parent (P-B), not the child.
- XML-doc-on-record-parameter CS1587 — documentation generation is off; `dotnet build` is 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- `TenantAuditSnapshot.PagingRecovered` — the type has no such member, so the GA sibling's `!result.PagingRecovered` guard is not applicable to the audit pager. (Its `_cursorHistory.Count > 0` guard *is* — see P-E.)
- `RunRefreshLoopAsync`'s restart-in-`finally` — traced for unbounded recursion; `_pending.Remove(key)` happens before the callbacks, so a restart requires a genuinely new notification. Bounded by inbound rate.
- `TenantUsersSnapshot.EmptyState` passing `false` positionally for `IsAuthorizationScopedEmpty` — verified correct; the terminal factories do not carry the authorization-safe-absence channel.
- `ContainsUntypeableCharacter` gaps for unpaired surrogates, private-use and unassigned code points — `EnumerateRunes` folds ill-formed sequences to U+FFFD and the residual categories render as visible tofu, which copy-paste confirmation satisfies.
- `GlobalAdministratorRemoveCommandSnapshot`'s mid-list positional parameter — type-incompatible with its neighbours, so any positional construction breaks at compile time; and `Hexalith.Tenants.UI` is not a published package.
- EN/FR resource parity — 1,227 keys each, zero asymmetry; all four new keys present in both files and referenced from live code.

#### Patch — derived from the resolved decisions (2026-08-01)

- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] [from D-H] Scope `stated_targets` to the declaration sections and make the last stated target authoritative — reuse the same section filter `declared_paths` applies (`scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py:110-114`) so prose, fenced code blocks and retained historical tables stop producing binding claims, and replace the per-path `set` + `any()` match (`:148-150`, `:280-285`) with an ordered last-wins target so a superseded row next to its correction cannot exonerate it. This must make `1-6-read-only-tenant-configuration.md` pass again on a correct tree while still failing a story whose authoritative row is stale. Fold in P-L (multi-path declaration lines, `:47-48`) and P-S (tokenizer: `|`/`*` stripping, non-overlapping chained arrows, en-dash `–>`), since all three change the same parse. [scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py:138]
- [x] [Review][Patch] [medium] [from D-I] Separate the locally-rejected route identity from a server-issued `400` — keep `NotFound` only for the identity this client refuses to escape before any request is sent, and route a `400` that actually came back from the server into the unavailable/degraded family with its raw code preserved, as the widened 5xx range already does. A server rejection is not evidence of non-existence and must not render as one on a caller-supplied string id. Add a test per arm proving the two are distinguishable. [src/Hexalith.Tenants.UI/Services/Gateways/TenantQueryGateway.cs:2818]

chunk: the gateway/client tests, refresh-subscription tests, state tests, UI composition tests, server
configuration tests, and generated-controller integration tests (11 files, +1,920/-102; 2,582 diff lines).

22/22; the Integration test project builds and its generated-controller class passes 27/27. This chunk's

### Review Findings — review loop 13, chunk B2 (component tests) + post-repair `src`/`scripts` delta (2026-08-01)

Four layers (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, acceptance-auditor) reviewed the remaining
never-reviewed executable surface: all 24 changed test files across `2cd7edf..working tree` plus the six
`src/` and `scripts/` files edited after loop 12's chunk-A review (30 files, +4,529/-257; 6,003 diff lines).
Chunk C (spec/evidence/`test-summary.md` prose) remains unreviewed.

The story gitlink validator passes with all six pointers `[declared]`, and the six declared targets match
`git ls-tree HEAD references/` exactly. The independent verification lane confirms the tree builds clean
(`dotnet build tests/Hexalith.Tenants.UI.Tests -c Release` → 0 warnings / 0 errors), the UI suite passes
1,820/1,820, EN/FR parity holds at 1,228 keys each, and both prohibited-symbol scans return no matches.
The compiler error this file's chunk-B1 section reports at `TenantDetailSurfaceTests.cs:4091` does not exist
in the tree under review.

Decision D-F's production side is confirmed correct at all five gate sites: the strict
`Lifecycle is not ProjectionLifecycleState.Current` test is retained and evaluated after the surface and
freshness clauses. The gap is in its evidence, not its implementation — see the first patch item below.

D-K accepted "no Python test lane for `validate-story-gitlinks.py`" as an owned limitation with manual executable verification. This loop found three defects in that script (raw-line arrow scanning, `Cf`-only stripping, and `File List` truncation), each of which a table-driven test would have caught, and two of which make the guard *pass a tree it should fail*. **Resolution (2026-08-01, review loop 13):** build the minimal test lane D-K declined. Manual verification is no longer sufficient for a guard whose failure mode is a silent pass. [scripts/validate-story-gitlinks.py]
